MANUAL DE EXCEL 2007

No exemplo pretendemos introduzir um cdigo nas clulas A9, A10 e A11 e obter automticamente a respectiva descrio e o correspondente preo unitrio.

Para tal iremos utilizar a funo Procv nas clulas B9:C11. No exemplo podemos ver a funo aplicada em B9 que seria copiada para B10 e B11.

NOTA: Ao utilizarmos a funo Procv necessitamos de ter em ateno os seguintes factores: 1. A pesquisa feita sempre na 1 coluna da tabela. 2. A 1 coluna da tabela, onde feita a pesquisa, deve estar ordenada por ordem crescente. 3. Ao seleccionarmos a tabela no devemos abranger a linha de cabealho, se esta existir. Aqui na janela da funo podemos ver a frmula que estaria na clula C9 e posteriormente copiada para C10 e C11.

Indicar o n da coluna onde est o resultado que pretendemos visualizar (Num_ndice_coluna). 4. (Procurar_intervalo) Este ltimo opcional e serve para indicar um Valor Lgico: 1 ou Verdadeiro, faz com que no caso de o valor introduzido na clula especificada em Valor_proc no existir seja colocado outro semelhante. 0 ou Falso s ser pesquisado um valor existente de facto na 1 coluna da tabela, caso contrrio surgir a indicao #N/D (Not Detected).

PROCH (HLOOKUP)
Esta funo basicamente idntica a Procv apenas a pesquisa, do contedo de uma determinada clula, realizada na 1 linha de uma tabela especificada, devolvendo em seguida um valor correspondente, numa outra linha indicada, da mesma tabela.

SE (IF)
Com base numa ou mais condies, devolve um valor se o teste condio for Verdadeiro ou outro se o teste for Falso. Sintaxe da Funo SE(teste_lgico; valor_se_verdadeiro; valor_se_falso)

Teste_lgico Condio ou condies que se pretendem testar. Pode ser um valor ou uma expresso. Quando existe a necessidade de combinar vrias condies recorre-se s funes lgicas E(AND) e OU(OR).

Exemplo de utilizao da funo SE.

Na coluna Resultado pretendemos obter uma informao referente ao aproveitamento do aluno. Para tal iremos verificar se a nota em B2 superior ou igual a 10 e se tal se verificar surgir o texto Aprovado, caso contrrio surgir o texto Reprovado.

E (AND)
As seguintes frases sero VERDADE ou MENTIRA? Tenho dois olhos E duas orelhas E nasci no planeta Terra. ou tem dois olhos E cinco orelhas E nasceu no planeta Terra. Resumindo: a funo E() para ser Verdadeira precisa que todas as opes sejam verdadeiras. E a funo ser assim: E(2+2=4;3+3=6) Portanto: verdade que 2+2=4 E tambm verdade que 3+3=6? Logo: E(10<100;5*5=25) E(10+90=100;5*5=25;4+4=8;10+1=175,4) A primeira verdade e a segunda falsa. Na prtica usamos o E() acoplado ao SE()

MANUAL DE EXCEL 2007

De acordo com o exemplo, vemos que a funo Soma.Se composta por 3 partes; rea onde iremos pesquisar (Intervalo) o nome do vendedor que nos inter<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

using ll = long long;

struct Point {
    int x, y;
    Point(int x_, int y_) : x(x_), y(y_) {}
};

bool is_collinear(const Point& a, const Point& b, const Point& c) {
    ll dx1 = b.x - a.x;
    ll dy1 = b.y - a.y;
    ll dx2 = c.x - a.x;
    ll dy2 = c.y - a.y;
    return dx1 * dy2 == dx2 * dy1;
}

bool is_between(const Point& a, const Point& b, const Point& c) {
    if (!is_collinear(a, b, c)) return false;
    ll dx = b.x - a.x;
    ll dy = b.y - a.y;
    ll dot = (c.x - a.x) * dx + (c.y - a.y) * dy;
    if (dot < 0) return false;
    ll len_sq = dx * dx + dy * dy;
    if (dot > len_sq) return false;
    return true;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    int k, n;
    cin >> k >> n;

    vector<Point> stones;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int x, y;
        cin >> x >> y;
        stones.emplace_back(x, y);
    }

    vector<Point> monsters;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int x, y;
        cin >> x >> y;
        monsters.emplace_back(x, y);
    }

    vector<vector<int>> pre_hit(n);
    for (int mi = 0; mi < n; ++mi) {
        const Point& m = monsters[mi];
        vector<int> hit;
        for (int si = 0; si < k; ++si) {
            const Point& s = stones[si];
            bool valid = true;
            for (int mj = 0; mj < n; ++mj) {
                if (mj == mi) continue;
                const Point& other = monsters[mj];
                if (is_collinear(s, m, other) && is_between(s, m, other)) {
                    valid = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
            if (valid) {
                hit.push_back(si);
            }
        }
        pre_hit[mi] = hit;
    }

    int ans = 0;
    for (int mi = 0; mi < n; ++mi) {
        const Point& m = monsters[mi];
        bool can_be_hit = false;
        for (int si = 0; si < k; ++si) {
            const Point& s = stones[si];
            vector<int> B;
            for (int mj = 0; mj < n; ++mj) {
                if (mj == mi) continue;
                const Point& other = monsters[mj];
                if (is_collinear(s, m, other) && is_between(s, m, other)) {
                    B.push_back(mj);
                }
            }
            bool valid = true;
            for (int b : B) {
                bool found = false;
                for (int s_b : pre_hit[b]) {
                    if (s_b != si) {
                        found = true;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (!found) {
                    valid = false;
                    break;
                }
            }
            if (valid && (B.size() + 1 <= k)) {
                can_be_hit = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (can_be_hit) {
            ans++;
        }
    }

    cout << ans << "\n";
    return 0;
}<#sep#> perhaps equation 2 can be diagonalized.

Equation 2:3y² +3yz + z²=4. Let's write the quadratic form matrix:

[3, 3/2]

[3/2,1]

Again, compute eigenvalues.

det( [3 - λ, 3/2; 3/2,1 - λ] )= (3 -λ)(1 -λ) - 9/4 = 3(1 -λ) -λ(1 -λ) -9/4 = 3 -3λ -λ +λ² -9/4= λ² -4λ +3 -9/4 = λ² -4λ + 3/4

Set equal to zero:

λ² -4λ + 3/4=0

Solutions: λ=(4 ± sqrt(16 -3))/2=(4 ±sqrt(13))/2=2 ± sqrt(13)/2

Sort all nodes in decreasing y-coordinates. Let them be ordered as v_0 (the root), v_1, v_2,...v_{n-1}

So the idea is:

Each node from v_1 to v_{n-1} must be connected into a tree by choosing a parent (among earlier nodes).

Each parent node can accept at most two children.

The total weight is the sum of (distance from parent to child) for all children.

The problem reduces to choosing the parents, such that the constraints on the number of children per parent are respected, and the total distance is minimal.

Now, how to optimally choose the parents?

It resembles an assignment problem with capacities. Each v_i (child candidate) must be assigned to exactly one parent, but the parents can only accept up to two children.

This brings to mind a variation of the minimum weight arborescence problem with capacities on parents' out-degrees.

The constraints: for each node except the root, exactly one parent. For each node, the number of children (i.e., how many children that node has assigned to it as a parent) is ≤2.

This is the problem.

This can be modeled as a flow problem.

The goal is to find an assignment (tree structure) with minimal total edge distance.

Alternatively, it's possible to model this as a flow problem.

Set up a flow network where edges are directed from possible parents to children, with capacity 1 (as each parent-child connection is either used or not). Each parent node in general has capacity 2 (cannot have more than two edges coming out of them).

The flow needs to connect every node (except the root) to their parent, forming a tree (arborescence). But ensuring that all these edges form a tree is complicated.

Alternatively, here's an idea inspired by the degree constraints:

We can model this as a graph where:

- All the edges possible for parent-child relations (from higher y to lower y) are considered as edges with weights (distance).

- We want to select a subset of these edges, forming an arborescence (so the directed edges form a tree from the root to all nodes), such that no node has more than two children.

Then, the minimal edge-weight sum is our answer.

This is exactly a minimum arborescence with degree constraints.

In literature, the problem of finding minimum arborescence with degree constraints on nodes is a known NP-hard problem, so for n up to 400 it might be difficult. However, binary trees may have some structure that allows us to find a solution.

+    def _compare(self, other, lower, upper, sigma):
+        """Actual comparison method for similar classes."""

+    def _checkCompareObj(self, other):
+        """Verify that the two objects are same class or subclasses."""
+        if not (isinstance(other, self.__class__) or
+                issubclass(other.__class__, self.__class__)):
+            oName = other.__class__.__name__
+            name = self.__class__.__name__
+            raise TypeError(
+                "Cannot compare against {} - not instance nor subclass "
+                "of {}".format(oName, name))
+
+    def _compareLogPreMsg(self, other, lower=None, upper=None, sigma=None,
+                          quantity=None):
+        """Log an INFO message about this specific comparison."""
+        leader = "Comparing {}> against < with the following tolerances:"
+        tols = [leader.format((quantity + ' from ') if quantity else ''), ]
+        for leader, obj in zip(('>', '<'), (self, other)):
+            tols.append("{} {}".format(leader, obj))
+        for title, val in zip(('Lower', 'Upper'), (lower, upper)):
+            if val is None:
+                continue
+            tols.append("{} tolerance: {:5.3F} [%]".format(title, val))
+        if sigma is not None:
+            sigmaStr = ("Confidence interval for statistical values: {:d} "
+                        "sigma or {} %")
+            sigmaDict = {1: 68, 2: 95}
+            tols.append(
+                sigmaStr.format(sigma, sigmaDict.get(sigma, '>= 99.7')
+                                if sigma else 0))
+        info('\n\t'.join(tols))

MANUAL DE EXCEL 2007 Funes financeiras

PGTO (PMT)
Calcula o valor da prestao para um dado capital com base numa determinada taxa de juro e por um perodo especfico. Sintaxe da Funo PGTO(taxa; nper; va; vf; tipo)

Taxa Taxa de juro. Se a prestao a calcular for mensal, ento a taxa de juro deve ser mensal.

Nper Nmero de perodos. Podem ser anos, meses, trimestres ou outra periodicidade.

Va Valor actual. Valor do capital inicial. Vf Valor futuro ou o saldo em dinheiro que deseja obter aps o ultimo pagamento ter sido efectuado, 0 (zero), se omisso. Tipo valor logico; pagamento no inicio do perodo =1; pagamento no final do perodo = 0 ou omisso.

Exemplo de utilizao da funo Pgto: Pretendemos saber quanto ficaremos a pagar, mensalmente, se adquirirmos uma habitao de 54.000 Euros, ficando em dvida 46.500 Euros a serem pagos em 30 anos a uma taxa anual de 9%.

20

MANUAL DE EXCEL 2007

Ao aplicar a funo, conforme est na clula B7, deveremos introduzir os seguintes dados.

Taxa = B4/12, onde B4 representa a taxa de juro anual, mas como pretendemos calcular a prestao mensal, essa taxa ter que ser mensal. Nper = B5*12, onde B5 representa os anos, obtendo assim o n total de pagamentos. Va = -B6, representa o valor em dvida (o sinal de opcional, depende apenas da utilizao que pretendemos dar ao aplicarmos a funo Pgto. Vf = Saldo que pretende obter aps ltimo pagamento (0 se omisso). Tipo = Valor lgico: 1 pagamento no incio do perodo 0 ou omisso, pagamento no final do perodo.

22

Formatao, proteo de dados e Formulrios

Formatao condicional
A formatao condicional ajuda a responder a questes especficas sobre dados. Pode aplicar a formatao condicional a um intervalo de clulas, a uma tabela do Excel ou a um relatrio de Tabela Dinmica. H diferenas importantes que devem ser entendidas ao usar formatao condicional num relatrio de Tabela Dinmica.

Os benefcios da formatao condicional ao analisar dados, ajuda a questes como:

Onde esto as excees num resumo de lucros nos ltimos cinco anos? Quais so as tendncias numa pesquisa de opinio de marketing ao longo dos ltimos dois anos? Quem vendeu mais do que 50.000 este ms? Qual a distribuio etria dos empregados? Quais so os alunos com melhor e pior desempenho na classe?

A formatao condicional ajuda a responder essas questes tornando mais fcil destacar clulas ou intervalos de clulas, enfatizar valores no-usuais e visualizar dados usando barras de dados, escalas de cores e conjuntos de cones. Um formato condicional altera a aparncia de um intervalo de clulas com base numa condio (ou critrio). Se a condio for verdadeira, o intervalo de clulas ser formatado com base nessa condio; se a condio for falsa, o intervalo de clulas no ser formatado com base nessa condio. Observao Quando criar um formato condicional, poder fazer referncia a outras clulas na mesma folha, mas no poder fazer referncia a clulas em outras folhas na mesma livro, ou utilizar referncias externas a outra livro. Escalas de cores so Menus visuais que ajudam a entender a distribuio e a variao de dados. Uma escala de duas cores ajuda a comparar um intervalo de clulas usando uma gradao de duas cores. O tom da cor representa valores maiores ou menores. Por exemplo, numa escala de cores verde e vermelha, pode especificar que clulas de valores mais altos tenham uma cor mais verde e clulas de valores mais baixos tenham uma cor mais vermelha. Problema: no vejo a minha formatao condicional para nenhuma clula no intervalo. Se uma ou mais clulas do intervalo, contiver uma frmula que retorna erro, a formatao condicional no ser aplicada ao intervalo inteiro. Para garantir que a formatao condicional seja aplicada ao intervalo inteiro, use uma funo ou SEERRO para retornar um valor diferente de erro.

3. Selecione uma escala de duas cores. Dica Passe o rato sobre os cones de escala de cores para ver qual deles corresponde a uma escala de duas cores. A cor da parte superior representa valores maiores; e a da parte inferior, valores menores. #include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Node {
    char type; // 'd' for digit, '<' or '>' for directions
    int value; // For digits, stores the current value
    int prev, next;
};

vector<int> process_query(const string& s, int l, int r) {
    int len = r - l + 1;
    if (len <= 0) return vector<int>(10, 0);
    vector<Node> nodes(len);
    for (int i = 0; i < len; ++i) {
        char c = s[l + i];
        if (isdigit(c)) {
            nodes[i].type = 'd';
            nodes[i].value = c - '0';
        } else {
            nodes[i].type = c;
            nodes[i].value = 0;
        }
        nodes[i].prev = i - 1;
        nodes[i].next = i + 1;
    }
    nodes[0].prev = -1;
    nodes[len - 1].next = -1;

    vector<int> ans(10, 0);
    int current = 0;
    bool dir_right = true; // true for right, false for left

    while (current != -1) {
        Node& node = nodes[current];
        if (node.type == 'd') {
            ans[node.value]++;
            node.value--;

            if (node.value < 0) {
                node.value = 0;
            }

            if (node.value == 0) {
                int prev = node.prev;
                int next = node.next;
                if (prev != -1) {
                    nodes[prev].next = next;
                }
                if (next != -1) {
                    nodes[next].prev = prev;
                }
                current = dir_right ? next : prev;
            } else {
                current = dir_right ? node.next : node.prev;
            }
        } else {
            dir_right = (node.type == '>');
            int next_node = dir_right ? node.next : node.prev;
            if (next_node == -1) {
                current = next_node;
                continue;
            }
            Node& next = nodes[next_node];
            if (next.type == '<' || next.type == '>') {
                int prev = node.prev;
                int next_next = dir_right ? next.next : next.prev;
                if (prev != -1) {
                    nodes[prev].next = next_node;
                }
                nodes[next_node].prev = prev;
                current = next_node;
                dir_right = (nodes[current].type == '>');
                next_node = dir_right ? nodes[current].next : nodes[current].prev;
                current = next_node;
            } else {
                current = next_node;
            }
        }
    }
    return ans;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);
    int n, q;
    cin >> n >> q;
    string s;
    cin >> s;
    while (q--) {
        int l, r;
        cin >> l >> r;
        l--; r--;
        vector<int> ans = process_query(s, l, r);
        for (int i = 0; i < 10; ++i) {
            cout << ans[i] << " \n"[i == 9];
        }
    }
    return 0;

The key points are:

- All edges are directed from parents (higher y) to children (lower y).

- The selection must form a tree.

- Each node's out-degree (number of children) is at most two.

The first thing is, for each node except the root, we can pick a parent. The parent must have fewer than two children so far.

To find the minimal cost, perhaps we can greedily pick the best (closest) edge, but making sure capacities are respected.

This would be similar to Krusky's algorithm:

Sort all possible parent-child edges (i.e, edges pointing from u to v where u has higher y) in increasing order of distance.

We then process edges in this order, including an edge only if it would not_exceed the parent's capacity (i.e., if the parent already has two children, skip this edge), and also it doesn't form a cycle (since it's a tree).

Wait here's the idea:

The edges are all possible from any u (with y higher than v's) to any v (higher y to lower y). Sort all these edges in increasing order of length.

We go through the edges from shortest to longest. We pick edges if they aren't creating a cycle and if the parent's children count doesn't exceed 2.

Thus, the selection must form a directed tree with root.

- Every node except root has exactly one parent in the set.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct ProcessResult {
    unordered_set<int> pre_hits;
    bool has_cycle;
    int cycle_start;
    int cycle_period;
};

ProcessResult simulate(int h_start, int x, int y, int a, int m) {
    unordered_map<int, int> visited;
    unordered_set<int> pre_hits;
    int current = h_start;
    int time = 0;

    while (true) {
        if (visited.find(current) != visited.end()) {
            int cycle_start = visited[current];
            int cycle_length = time - cycle_start;
            bool found = false;
            int cycle_hit_time = -1;

            for (const auto& entry : visited) {
                if (entry.first == a && entry.second >= cycle_start) {
                    found = true;
                    cycle_hit_time = entry.second;
                    break;
                }
            }

            if (found) {
                return {pre_hits, true, cycle_hit_time, cycle_length};
            } else {
                return {pre_hits, false, 0, 0};
            }
        }

        visited[current] = time;
        if (current == a) {
            pre_hits.insert(time);
        }

        current = (x * current + y) % m;
        time++;
    }
}

tuple<int, int, int> extended_gcd(int a, int b) {
    if (b == 0) {
        return {a, 1, 0};
    } else {
        auto [g, x, y] = extended_gcd(b, a % b);
        return {g, y, x - (a / b) * y};
    }
}

int mod_inverse(int a, int m) {
    auto [g, x, y] = extended_gcd(a, m);
    if (g != 1) {
        return -1;
    } else {
        return (x % m + m) % m;
    }
}

pair<bool, int> crt(int a, int m, int b, int n) {
    int d = gcd(m, n);
    int c = b - a;
    if (c % d != 0) {
        return {false, 0};
    }

    int m_prime = m / d;
    int n_prime = n / d;
    c /= d;

    int inv = mod_inverse(m_prime, n_prime);
    if (inv == -1) {
        return {false, 0};
    }

    int k0 = (c % n_prime) * inv % n_prime;
    if (k0 < 0) {
        k0 += n_prime;
    }

    int x0 = a + k0 * m;
    int mod = m / d * n;
    x0 %= mod;
    if (x0 < 0) {
        x0 += mod;
    }

    return {true, x0};
}

bool is_in(int t, const ProcessResult& res) {
    if (res.pre_hits.count(t)) {
        return true;
    }
    if (res.has_cycle && t >= res.cycle_start) {
        if ((t - res.cycle_start) % res.cycle_period == 0) {
            return true;
        }
    }
    return false;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int m;
    cin >> m;

    int h1, a1, x1, y1;
    cin >> h1 >> a1 >> x1 >> y1;

    int h2, a2, x2, y2;
    cin >> h2 >> a2 >> x2 >> y2;

    ProcessResult x_res = simulate(h1, x1, y1, a1, m);
    ProcessResult y_res = simulate(h2, x2, y2, a2, m);

    int ans = INT_MAX;

    for (int t : x_res.pre_hits) {
        if (is_in(t, y_res)) {
            ans = min(ans, t);
        }
    }

    for (int t : y_res.pre_hits) {
        if (is_in(t, x_res)) {
            ans = min(ans, t);
        }
    }

    if (x_res.has_cycle && y_res.has_cycle) {
        auto [exists, t0] = crt(x_res.cycle_start, x_res.cycle_period, y_res.cycle_start, y_res.cycle_period);
        if (exists) {
            int lower = max(x_res.cycle_start, y_res.cycle_start);
            int mod = (x_res.cycle_period / gcd(x_res.cycle_period, y_res.cycle_period)) * y_res.cycle_period;

            if (t0 < lower) {
                int delta = lower - t0;
                int k = (delta + mod - 1) / mod;
                t0 += k * mod;
            }

            if (t0 >= lower) {
                ans = min(ans, t0);
            }
        }
    }

    if (ans == INT_MAX) {
        cout << -1 << endl;
    } else {
        cout << ans << endl;
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> [ other terms ]

So the idea is:

Sort all possible edges (u, v) where u is any node with higher y-coordinate than v, in order of increasing length.

Process each edge in this order. For each (u, v), if:

- v hasn't been parented yet, and

- u's current child count is less than 2,

then we can use this edge: set v's parent to u, decrement u's remaining slots (count up how many children it has).

Proceed until all nodes except root are parented. If after processing all edges, any nodes remain unparented, output -1. Otherwise, the total sum is the answer.

Thus, the algorithm could work.

This suggests that proceeding greedily in order of shortest edges and taking them whenever possible by checking available slots may work.

Let me formalize this approach:

Algorithm steps:

1. Check that there is exactly one node with maximum y. (Otherwise output -1).

2. Sort all nodes in decreasing order of y-coordinate. Let the root node be the first node.

3. Build a list of all candidate edges from any node u to any node v where y[u] > y[v].

   For each pair (u, v) with y_u > y_v, add the edge (u, v) with weight (distance).

   Also, since the parent must have higher y, any edge must have u has higher y than v.

4. Sort all these edges in increasing order of distance (smallest first).

5. Proceed to select edges in order:

   - Each node (except the root) must be assigned exactly one parent, which is among the nodes that are already considered as parents (i.e. in the processed nodes with higher y).

   We need methods to track:

   • For each node, whether it's been assigned a parent.

   • For each potential parent (u), how many children have already been assigned to them.

   Data structures involved:

   • An array 'parent', where parent[v] records the parent node of v, or null if not yet assigned.

   • An array 'children_count' where children_count[u] is the number of children already assigned to node u.

6. Initialize the parent for all nodes to null except the root (parent is set to 'none', but not required).

   children_count starts as 0 for all nodes.

7. For each edge (u,v) in the sorted list:

   if v has no parent yet.

   and children_count[u] <2.

   then:

      assign parent[v] = u.

      children_count[u] +=1.

8. After processing all edges:

   check whether all nodes except the root have a parent.

   If yes, compute total of distance for all edges chosen.

   Else output -1.

This approach could work! Because it greedily assigns edges, always taking the minimal edges first, as long as they can be attached without conflict.

The question is, will this always yield the minimal total length? Let's think about possible counter examples.

Suppose there are three nodes: root (R) and two more nodes, A and B, both below R.

Suppose the distances are R-A = 1, R-B= 3. A-B= 0.5.

edges from R to A is length 1,

R to B: 3,

+    def _compare(self, other, lower, upper, _sigma):
+
+        similar = self._compareMetadata(other, lower, upper, _sigma)
+        if not self._comparePrecheckMetadata(other):
+            return False
+        similar &= self._compareMaterials(other, lower, upper, _sigma)
+        return similar
+
+    def _comparePrecheckMetadata(self, other):
+        for key, myVec in iteritems(self.metadata):
+            otherVec = other.metadata[key]
+            if len(myVec) != len(otherVec):
+                error("Stopping comparison early due to mismatched {} vectors"
+                      "\n\t>{}\n\t<{}".format(key, myVec, otherVec))
+                return False
+        return True
+
+    @compareDocDecorator
+    def compareMaterials(self, other, lower=DEF_COMP_LOWER,
+                         upper=DEF_COMP_UPPER, sigma=DEF_COMP_SIGMA):
+        """
+        Return the result of comparing all materials on two readers
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        other: :class:`DepletionReader`
+            Reader to compare against
+        {compLimits}
+        {sigma}
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        bool:
+            ``True`` if all materials agree to the given tolerances
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        {compTypeErr}
+        """
+        self._checkCompareObj(other)
+        self._compareLogPreMsg(other, lower, upper, quantity='materials')
+
+        if not self._comparePrecheckMetadata(other):
+            return False
+
+        return self._compareMaterials(other, lower, upper, sigma)
+
+    def _compareMaterials(self, other, lower, upper, sigma):
+        """Private method for going directly into the comparison."""
+        commonMats = getKeyMatchingShapes(
+            self.materials, other.materials, 'materials')
+        similar = (
+            len(self.materials) == len(other.materials) == len(commonMats))
+
+        for matName in sorted(commonMats):
+            myMat = self[matName]
+            otherMat = other[matName]
+            similar &= myMat.compare(otherMat, lower, upper, sigma)
+        return similar
+
+    @compareDocDecorator
+    def compareMetadata(self, other, lower=DEF_COMP_LOWER,
+                        upper=DEF_COMP_UPPER, sigma=DEF_COMP_SIGMA):
+        """
+        Return the result of comparing metadata on two readers
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        other: :class:`DepletionReader`
+            Object to compare against
+        {compLimits}
+        {header}
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        bool
+            True if the metadata agree within the given tolerances
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        {compTypeErr}
+        """
+
+        self._checkCompareObj(other)
+
+        self._compareLogPreMsg(other, lower, upper, quantity='metadata')
+
+        return self._compareMetadata(other, lower, upper, sigma)
+
+    def _compareMetadata(self, other, lower, upper, _sigma):
+        """Private method for comparing metadata"""
+
+        similar = logDirectCompare(
+            self.metadata['names'], other.metadata['names'],
+            0, 0, 'names')
+        similar &= logDirectCompare(
+            self.metadata['zai'], other.metadata['zai'],
+            0, 0, 'zai')
+        similar &= logDirectCompare(
+            self.metadata['days'], other.metadata['days'],
+            lower, upper, 'days')
+        similar &= logDirectCompare(
+            self.metadata['burnup'], other.metadata['burnup'],
+            lower, upper, 'burnup')
+        return similar

Criar um relatrio de grfico dinmico a partir de<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Node {
    int l, r;
    mutable long long val;
    Node(int l_, int r_, long long val_) : l(l_), r(r_), val(val_) {}
    bool operator<(const Node& rhs) const { return l < rhs.l; }
};

set<Node> intervals;

long long powmod(long long a, long long b, long long mod) {
    a %= mod;
    long long res = 1;
    while (b > 0) {
        if (b & 1) res = res * a % mod;
        a = a * a % mod;
        b >>= 1;
    }
    return res;
}

auto split(int pos) {
    auto it = intervals.lower_bound(Node(pos, 0, 0));
    if (it != intervals.end() && it->l == pos) return it;
    it--;
    int l = it->l, r = it->r;
    long long v = it->val;
    intervals.erase(it);
    intervals.insert(Node(l, pos-1, v));
    return intervals.insert(Node(pos, r, v)).first;
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m, vmax;
    long long seed;
    cin >> n >> m >> seed >> vmax;

    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        long long current = seed;
        seed = (seed * 7 + 13) % 1000000007;
        intervals.insert(Node(i, i, (current % vmax) + 1));
    }

    auto rnd = [&]() {
        long long ret = seed;
        seed = (seed * 7 + 13) % 1000000007;
        return ret;
    };

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int op = rnd() % 4 + 1;
        int l = rnd() % n + 1;
        int r = rnd() % n + 1;
        if (l > r) swap(l, r);

        int x, y = 0;
        if (op == 3) {
            x = rnd() % (r - l + 1) + 1;
        } else {
            x = rnd() % vmax + 1;
        }
        if (op == 4) {
            y = rnd() % vmax + 1;
        }

        if (op == 1) {
            auto it_r = split(r + 1);
            auto it_l = split(l);
            for (auto it = it_l; it != it_r; ++it) {
                it->val += x;
            }
        } else if (op == 2) {
            auto it_r = split(r + 1);
            auto it_l = split(l);
            intervals.erase(it_l, it_r);
            auto new_it = intervals.insert(Node(l, r, x)).first;

            // Merge left
            while (new_it != intervals.begin()) {
                auto prev_it = prev(new_it);
                if (prev_it->val == new_it->val) {
                    Node merged(prev_it->l, new_it->r, new_it->val);
                    intervals.erase(prev_it);
                    intervals.erase(new_it);
                    new_it = intervals.insert(merged).first;
                } else break;
            }

            // Merge right
            while (true) {
                auto next_it = next(new_it);
                if (next_it != intervals.end() && next_it->val == new_it->val) {
                    Node merged(new_it->l, next_it->r, new_it->val);
                    intervals.erase(next_it);
                    intervals.erase(new_it);
                    new_it = intervals.insert(merged).first;
                } else break;
            }
        } else if (op == 3) {
            vector<pair<long long, int>> vec;
            auto it_r = split(r + 1);
            auto it_l = split(l);
            for (auto it = it_l; it != it_r; ++it) {
                vec.emplace_back(it->val, it->r - it->l + 1);
            }
            sort(vec.begin(), vec.end());
            int sum = 0;
            for (auto &p : vec) {
                sum += p.second;
                if (sum >= x) {
                    cout << p.first << '\n';
                    break;
                }
            }
        } else if (op == 4) {
            long long sum = 0;
            auto it_r = split(r + 1);
            auto it_l = split(l);
            for (auto it = it_l; it != it_r; ++it) {
                long long val = it->val;
                int cnt = it->r - it->l + 1;
                sum = (sum + powmod(val, x, y) * cnt) % y;
            }
            cout << sum % y << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;

m= [1 + (5/4)z²] -ab + [(16 -z²)/12] - (5a z)/2 +2b z +7/4 z²

Simplify:

Combine constants:1 +16/12=1 +4/3=7/3

Combine z² terms:5/4 z² -1/12 z² +7/4 z²= (5/4 +7/4 -1/12) z²= (12/4 -1/12) z²= (3 -1/12) z²= (35/12) z²

So m=7/3 + (35/12) z² -ab - (5a z)/2 +2b z

Now, substitute a and b:

a=±sqrt(1 + (5/4)z²)

b=±sqrt( (16 -z²)/12 )

But ab= (±)(±) sqrt(1 +5/4 z²) sqrt( (16 -z²)/12 )

So ab= ± sqrt( [1 +5/4 z²][(16 -z²)/12 ] )

Thus, the term -ab can be either negative or positive depending on the sign of a and b. Similarly, - (5a z)/2 +2b z involves the signs of a and b.

This seems too variable, but perhaps the maximum and minimum of m can be found by considering different combinations of signs for a and b. However, this is very involved.

Alternatively, consider choosing the signs of a and b to maximize or minimize the expression for m. For example, to minimize m, we might want to choose ab to be positive (thus making -ab as negative as possible), and to choose - (5a z)/2 +2b z as negative as possible. Conversely, to maximize m, choose ab to be negative (making -ab positive), and -5a z /2 +2b z as positive as possible.

But this is quite heuristic. Maybe, instead of considering all combinations, look for cases where a and b are both positive, both negative, or mixed.

But without loss of generality, maybe assume z is positive (since the problem is symmetric in z? Wait, no, the equations involve terms like xz and yz, which are not symmetric if z is replaced by -z. So maybe not symmetric.

Alternatively, consider z ≥0 and handle z ≤0 separately. But this could double the work.

This problem seems quite difficult. Maybe a numerical approach would work, but since this is a theoretical problem, we need an analytical solution.

Alternatively, think of the third equation as the squared norm in some transformed coordinates, and try to find the minimal and maximal possible m by analyzing the constraints from the first two equations.

Alternatively, use the method of Lagrange multipliers with multiple variables. Let's attempt that.

We need to find the extrema of m =x² -xy + y² subject to the constraints:

1. x² +3xz + z² =1

2.3y² +3yz + z²=4

Set up the Lagrangian:

L = x² -xy + y² - λ1(x² +3xz + z² -1) -λ2(3y² +3yz + z² -4)

Take partial derivatives:

∂L/∂x = 2x - y -λ1(2x +3z) =0

∂L/∂y = -x + 2y -λ2(6y +3z)=0

∂L/∂z = -λ1(3x +2z) -λ2(3y +2z) =0

Constraints:

x² +3xz + z²=1

3y² +3yz + z²=4

This gives us four equations plus the two constraints, which is a system of six equations with variables x, y, z, λ1, λ2.

This system might be solvable. Let's attempt.

From ∂L/∂x=0:

2x - y = λ1(2x +3z) → (1) 2x - y = λ1(2x +3z)

From ∂L/∂y=0:

-x +2y = λ2(6y +3z) → (2) -x +2y = λ2(6y +3z)

From ∂L/∂z=0:

-λ1(3x +2z) -λ2(3y +2z)=0 → (3) λ1(3x +2z) + λ2(3y +2z)=0

And the two constraints:

(4) x² +3xz + z²=1

(5) 3y² +3yz + z²=4

This system looks complicated, but perhaps we can express λ1 and λ2 from (1) and (2) and substitute into (3).

From (1):

λ1 = (2x - y)/(2x +3z)

From (2):

λ2= (-x +2y)/(6y +3z)= ( -x +2y )/(3(2y + z) )

Substitute λ1 and λ2 into (3):

( (2x - y)/(2x +3z) )(3x +2z) + ( (-x +2y)/(3(2y + z)) )(3y +2z)=0

Simplify:

First term:

(2x - y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z)

Second term:

(-x +2y)(3y +2z)/(3(2y + z))

Note that 3y +2z =3(y) +2z and 2y + z. Not sure if they relate.

Let me compute both terms:

First term:

Multiply numerator: (2x - y)(3x +2z) =6x² +4xz -3xy -2yz

Denominator:2x +3z

Second term:

Multiply numerator: (-x +2y)(3y +2z) =-3xy -2xz +6y² +4yz

Denominator:3(2y + z)

So equation (3) becomes:

[6x² +4xz -3xy -2yz]/(2x +3z) + [ -3xy -2xz +6y² +4yz ]/[3(2y + z) ]=0

This is very complex. Let's denote equation (3) as A + B =0, where A is the first fraction and B the second.

Hmm, perhaps factor out terms. Let's see.

First term numerator:6x² +4xz -3xy -2yz

=6x² +4xz - y(3x +2z)

Denominator:2x +3z

Second term numerator:-3xy -2xz +6y² +4yz

=-x(3y +2z) +2y(3y +2z)

=( -x +2y )(3y +2z )

Denominator:3(2y +z )

Thus, second term is [ (-x +2y)(3y +2z) ]/[3(2y +z) ]

But from equation (2), λ2 = (-x +2y)/[3(2y + z) ]

But I don't know if that helps.

Alternatively, let's note that (-x +2y) is the numerator of λ2 multiplied by 3(2y +z)

But this might not help.

Alternatively, perhaps express the first term as follows:

First term:

[6x² +4xz -3xy -2yz]/(2x +3z) = [6x² +4xz - y(3x +2z)]/(2x +3z)

Maybe factor numerator:

Let me check if 6x² +4xz -3xy -2yz can be factored.

Group terms:

6x² +4xz and -3xy -2yz

Factor 2x from first pair:2x(3x +2z)

Factor -y from second pair:-y(3x +2z)

Thus, the numerator is (2x -y)(3x +2z)

Therefore, first term is (2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z)

Thus, equation (3) becomes:

+    def _compare(self, other, lower, upper, sigma):
+        similar = self.compareMetadata(other)
+        similar &= self.compareResults(other, lower, upper, sigma)
+        similar &= self.compareUniverses(other, lower, upper, sigma)
+        return similar

+    @compareDocDecorator
+    def compareMetadata(self, other, header=False):
+        """
+        Return True if the metadata (settings) are identical.
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        other: :class:`ResultsReader`
+            Class against which to compare
+        {header}
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        bool:
+            If the metadata are identical
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        {compTypeErr}
+        """
+
+        self._checkCompareObj(other)
+        if header:
+            self._compareLogPreMsg(other, quantity='metadata')
+        myKeys = set(self.metadata.keys())
+        otherKeys = set(other.metadata.keys())
+        similar = not any(myKeys.symmetric_difference(otherKeys))
+        commonKeys = getCommonKeys(myKeys, otherKeys, 'metadata')
+        skips = commonKeys.intersection(self.__METADATA_COMP_SKIPS)
+        if any(skips):
+            info("The following items will be skipped in the comparison\n\t{}"
+                 .format(', '.join(sorted(skips))))
+        for key in sorted(commonKeys):
+            if key in self.__METADATA_COMP_SKIPS:
+                continue
+            selfV = self.metadata[key]
+            otherV = other.metadata[key]
+            similar &= logDirectCompare(selfV, otherV, 0., 0., key)
+
+        return similar
+
+    @compareDocDecorator
+    def compareResults(self, other, lower=DEF_COMP_LOWER,
+                       upper=DEF_COMP_UPPER, sigma=DEF_COMP_SIGMA,
+                       header=False):
+        """
+        Compare the contents of the results dictionary
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        other: :class:`ResultsReader`
+            Class against which to compare
+        {compLimits}
+        {sigma}
+        {header}
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        bool:
+            If the results data agree to given tolerances
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        {compTypeErr}
+        """
+        self._checkCompareObj(other)
+        if header:
+            self._compareLogPreMsg(other, lower, upper, sigma, 'results')
+        myRes = self.resdata
+        otherR = other.resdata
+
+        commonTypeKeys = getKeyMatchingShapes(myRes, otherR, 'results')
+
+        similar = len(commonTypeKeys) == len(myRes) == len(otherR)
+
+        for key in sorted(commonTypeKeys):
+            mine = myRes[key]
+            theirs = otherR[key]
+            if key in RES_DATA_NO_UNCS:
+                similar &= logDirectCompare(mine, theirs, lower, upper, key)
+                continue
+            myVals, myUncs = splitValsUncs(mine)
+            theirVals, theirUncs = splitValsUncs(theirs)
+            similar &= getLogOverlaps(key, myVals, theirVals, myUncs,
+                                      theirUncs, sigma, relative=True)
+        return similar
+
+    @compareDocDecorator
+    def compareUniverses(self, other, lower=DEF_COMP_LOWER,
+                         upper=DEF_COMP_UPPER, sigma=DEF_COMP_SIGMA):
+        """
+        Compare the contents of the ``universes`` dictionary
+
+        Parameters
+        ----------
+        other: :class:`ResultsReader`
+            Reader by which to compare
+        {compLimits}
+        {sigma}
+
+        Returns
+        -------
+        bool:
+            If the contents of the universes agree to given tolerances
+
+        Raises
+        ------
+        {compTypeErr}
+        """
+        self._checkCompareObj(other)
+        myUniverses = self.universes
+        otherUniverses = other.universes
+        keyGoodTypes = getKeyMatchingShapes(myUniverses, otherUniverses,
+                                            'universes')
+
+        similar = len(keyGoodTypes) == len(myUniverses) == len(otherUniverses)
+
+        for univKey in keyGoodTypes:
+            myUniv = myUniverses[univKey]
+            otherUniv = otherUniverses[univKey]
+            similar &= myUniv.compare(otherUniv, lower, upper, sigma)
+        return similar

Macros e Edio VBA

O que uma Macro

Caso execute uma tarefa vrias vezes no Microsoft Excel, possvel automatiz-la com uma macro. Uma macro uma sequncia de comandos e funes armazenados em um mdulo do Visual Basic for Applications - VBA e pode ser executada sempre que voc precisar executar a tarefa. Quando voc grava uma macro, o Excel armazena informaes sobre cada etapa realizada medida que voc executa uma sequncia de comandos. Em seguida, voc executa a macro para repetir, ou "reproduzir", os comandos.

Por exemplo, vamos supor que, seguidamente precisa formatar uma clula com Negrito, cor de fonte Vermelha, Itlico, Fonte Verdana de Tamanho 13 com quebra automtica de linha. Ao invs de ter que executar todos os comandos de formatao em cada clula, pode criar uma Macro que aplica todos os comandos de formatao. Aps criada a Macro, cada vez que tiver que aplicar o conjunto de comandos de formatao, basta executar a Macro, o que normalmente feito atravs da associao de uma combinao de teclas com a Macro, como por exemplo Ctrl+L. No nosso exemplo, cada vez que quiser formatar uma clula com os formatos descritos, basta clicar na clula e pressionar Ctrl+L. Mais fcil do que aplicar cada comando individualmente.

35

MANUAL DE EXCEL 2007

Antes de gravar uma macro, planeie as etapas e os comandos que deseja que a macro execute. Se cometer um erro durante a gravao da macro, as correes feitas tambm so gravadas. Ao gravar macros, o VBA armazena cada macro em um novo mdulo anexado a uma pasta de trabalho.

Gravar e executar uma Macro

Quando grava uma macro, o gravador de macro grava todas as etapas necessrias para concluir as aes a serem executadas por essa macro. A navegao na Faixa de Opes no includa nas etapas gravadas. Observao A Faixa de Opes um componente da Interface de utilizador do Microsoft Office Fluent. Primeiramente o menu Programador deve estar ativado, aparecendo na barra de menus, caso no esteja:

1. Se o Menu Programador no estiver disponvel, faa o seguinte: 1. Clique no Boto Microsoft Office Excel. e, em seguida, clique em Opes do

2. Na categoria Popular, em Opes principais para trabalhar com o Excel, marque a caixa de seleo Mostrar Separador Programador no Friso e clique em OK. 2. Para definir o nvel de segurana temporariamente e habilitar todas as macros, faa o seguinte: 1. Na Menu Programador, no grupo Cdigo, clique em Segurana de Macros.

O Editor de Visual Basic

O Editor de Visual Basic um ambiente de desenvolvimento que possibilita trabalhar com formulrios e objetos. Serve para que possamos atravs deste formulrios e objetos criar cdigo de programao para facilitar a criao de solues de uma maneira mais significativa. o ambiente de desenvolvimento e edio de macros disponibilizado nos aplicativos do Microsoft Office. Permite ainda Editar ou altera macros existentes ou previamente criadas. Esta disponvel no Menu Programador junto ao Boto de Macros.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;
const int MAXN = 1e5 + 10;

long long fact[MAXN];

void precompute_factorials() {
    fact[0] = 1;
    for (int i = 1; i < MAXN; ++i) {
        fact[i] = fact[i - 1] * i % MOD;
    }
}

struct CheckResult {
    bool valid;
    int components;
    int total_nodes;
};

CheckResult check_subset(const vector<pair<int, int>>& edges) {
    unordered_map<int, vector<int>> adj;
    unordered_set<int> nodes;

    for (const auto& e : edges) {
        int u = e.first, v = e.second;
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
        nodes.insert(u);
        nodes.insert(v);
    }

    for (int node : nodes) {
        if (adj[node].size() > 2) {
            return {false, 0, 0};
        }
    }

    unordered_set<int> visited;
    int components = 0;
    bool valid = true;

    for (int node : nodes) {
        if (visited.count(node)) continue;
        components++;
        queue<int> q;
        q.push(node);
        visited.insert(node);
        int m = 0;
        int sum_degree = 0;

        while (!q.empty()) {
            int u = q.front();
            q.pop();
            m++;
            sum_degree += adj[u].size();

            for (int v : adj[u]) {
                if (!visited.count(v)) {
                    visited.insert(v);
                    q.push(v);
                }
            }
        }

        if (sum_degree % 2 != 0 || sum_degree / 2 != m - 1) {
            valid = false;
        }
    }

    if (!valid) {
        return {false, 0, 0};
    }

    return {true, components, (int)nodes.size()};
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    precompute_factorials();

    int T;
    cin >> T;

    while (T--) {
        int N, M;
        cin >> N >> M;
        vector<pair<int, int>> edges(M);
        for (int i = 0; i < M; ++i) {
            int u, v;
            cin >> u >> v;
            if (u > v) swap(u, v);
            edges[i] = {u, v};
        }

        long long ans = 0;

        for (int mask = 0; mask < (1 << M); ++mask) {
            vector<pair<int, int>> subset;
            for (int i = 0; i < M; ++i) {
                if (mask & (1 << i)) {
                    subset.push_back(edges[i]);
                }
            }

            auto [valid, components, total_nodes] = check_subset(subset);
            if (!valid) continue;

            int cnt = __builtin_popcount(mask);
            int m_blocks = N - total_nodes + components;

            if (m_blocks < 0) continue;
            if (m_blocks >= MAXN) {
                // Handle cases where m_blocks exceeds precomputed factorials (unlikely as N is 1e5)
                // For this problem, N is up to 1e5 and M up to 7, so m_blocks is up to 1e5 + 7, which is covered by MAXN
                continue;
            }

            long long pow2 = 1LL << components;
            pow2 %= MOD;

            long long term = fact[m_blocks] * pow2 % MOD;
            if (cnt % 2 == 1) {
                term = MOD - term;
            }

            ans = (ans + term) % MOD;
        }

        cout << (ans % MOD + MOD) % MOD << '\n';
    }

    return 0;

Therefore, first term is (2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z)

Thus, equation (3) becomes:

(2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z) + [ (-x +2y)(3y +2z) ]/[3(2y +z) ] =0

This simplifies equation (3) to:

(2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z) + [ (-x +2y)(3y +2z) ]/[3(2y +z) ] =0

This seems a bit more manageable. Let me denote term1 and term2:

term1 = (2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z)

term2 = [ (-x +2y)(3y +2z) ]/[3(2y +z) ]

Then, term1 + term2 =0 → term1 = -term2

Thus,

(2x -y)(3x +2z)/(2x +3z) = - [ (-x +2y)(3y +2z) ]/[3(2y +z) ]

Multiply both sides by (2x +3z)*3(2y +z) to eliminate denominators:

3(2x -y)(3x +2z)(2y +z) + (x -2y)(3y +2z)(2x +3z)=0

Expand each term:

First term:3(2x -y)(3x +2z)(2y +z)

Second term:(x -2y)(3y +2z)(2x +3z)

This expansion would be extremely lengthy. Perhaps there is a better approach. Let's see if we can find some relation between variables.

Alternatively, assume that z=0. Is that possible?

If z=0, check if it satisfies the constraints.

From equation 1: x²=1→x=±1

From equation 2:3y²=4→y=±2/√3≈±1.1547

From equation 3:m=x² -xy +y²=1 - x*(±2/√3) + (4/3)

If x=1, then m=1 - (2/√3) +4/3≈1 +1.333 -1.1547≈1.178

If x=-1, then m=1 + (2/√3) +4/3≈1 +1.333 +1.1547≈3.487

So for z=0, m can be approximately 1.178 or 3.487. But since we need real solutions, these are possible m values. But we need to check if these are the extrema.

But maybe z=0 is not the only case. What if z takes other values?

Alternatively, consider symmetry. If we set z= t, then for each t in [-4,4], we can compute x and y with possible signs, and then compute m. The range of m would then be the union over all t.

Alternatively, consider the maximum and minimum of m(z) over z ∈ [-4,4]. But the expression is too complicated. Maybe look for critical points by taking derivative of m(z) and setting it to zero. But m(z) is expressed in terms of sqrt(5z²+4) and sqrt(48-3z²), which complicates differentiation.

z=2:

From equation 1: x² +6x +4 =1 →x² +6x +3=0→Discriminant=36-12=24>0→x=(-6±sqrt(24))/2= -3±sqrt(6)

From equation 2:3y² +6y +4=4→3y² +6y=0→3y(y +2)=0→y=0 or y=-2

Case 1: x= -3 + sqrt(6), y=0

m= x² -0 +0= x²= ( -3 + sqrt(6) )²=9 -6 sqrt(6) +6=15 -6 sqrt(6)≈15-14.696=0.304

Case 2: x= -3 - sqrt(6), y=0

m= x²= ( -3 - sqrt(6) )²=9 +6 sqrt(6) +6=15 +6 sqrt(6)≈15+14.696≈29.696

Case 3: x= -3 + sqrt(6), y=-2

m= x² -x*(-2) + (-2)^2= x² +2x +4= (15 -6 sqrt(6)) + 2*(-3 + sqrt(6)) +4=15-6√6 -6 +2√6 +4=13 -4√6≈13-9.798≈3.202

Case 4: x= -3 - sqrt(6), y=-2

m= x² -x*(-2) +4= x² +2x +4= (15 +6√6) +2*(-3 -√6) +4=15+6√6 -6 -2√6 +4=13 +4√6≈13+9.798≈22.798

So for z=2, possible m values: ~0.304, ~29.696, ~3.202, ~22.798

Interesting. Previously, for z=0, m was around 1.178 and 3.487. So with z=2, there's a lower m of ~0.304 and higher m of ~29.696. This suggests that m can range from as low as approximately 0.3 to as high as ~29.7.

Another test with z=4. However, z=4 is the maximum allowed value.

For z=4:

From equation 1: x² +12x +16=1→x² +12x +15=0→Discriminant=144-60=84→x=(-12±sqrt(84))/2=-6±sqrt(21)

From equation 2:3y² +12y +16=4→3y² +12y +12=0→y² +4y +4=0→(y+2)^2=0→y=-2

So for z=4, y must be -2. Then, x=-6±sqrt(21)

Compute m for each x:

x=-6+sqrt(21), y=-2

m= x² -x*(-2) + (-2)^2= x² +2x +4

= (36 -12 sqrt(21) +21) +2*(-6 + sqrt(21)) +4

=57 -12 sqrt(21) -12 +2 sqrt(21) +4

=49 -10 sqrt(21)≈49-45.837≈3.163

x=-6 -sqrt(21), y=-2

m= x² +2x +4

= (36 +12 sqrt(21) +21) +2*(-6 -sqrt(21)) +4

=57 +12 sqrt(21) -12 -2 sqrt(21) +4

=49 +10 sqrt(21)≈49+45.837≈94.837

But z=4 gives m≈3.163 and ≈94.837. However, wait, in this case, for z=4, m can be 3.163 or 94.837, which is higher than previous maxima.

But wait, equation 2 requires that for z=4, sqrt(48 -3*16)=sqrt(48-48)=0. So y is real only as computed.

But in this case, m reaches up to ~94.837 for z=4, x=-6 -sqrt(21), y=-2.

Similarly, check z=-4.

For z=-4:

From equation1: x² -12x +16=1→x² -12x +15=0→Discriminant=144-60=84→x=(12±sqrt(84))/2=6±sqrt(21)

From equation2:3y² -12y +16=4→3y² -12y +12=0→y² -4y +4=0→(y-2)^2=0→y=2

Then,<#sep#>This seems like the problem requires that the parent of a node must be chosen before its children's edges are considered.

This implies that nodes must be processed depth-first, ensuring that a node's parent comes before it in whatever processing order.

Alternatively, we could represent this as the edge from the parent appears before the edges from child nodes.

But how?

Alternatively, maybe the algorithm should sort edges not just on distance, but also in a way where edges incident to parents with higher y are processed first.

Wait the edges are already in order of distance.

Wait, perhaps the correct approach is to process the edges in order of increasing distance, but whenever considering an edge (u, v) (parent u, child v):

- u must be able to act as a parent (its child count not full), and

- v does not have a parent, and 

- the parent node u must have been already selected (i.e., has at least one edge (its own parent) to the tree).

Wait but how is that tracked?

This feels like a problem that may require a priority queue and ensuring that nodes are processed in some order based on their connection to the root.

Hmm, perhaps we can use a modified greedy algorithm.

Here's an alternative plan:

Each node except the root must be connected to a parent. The parent must be on higher levels (previous nodes). We can model this as follows:

We can use a priority queue where we consider, for each node not yet connected, the closest available parent.

This is more like the Prim's algorithm.

In this case, Prim’s algorithm could work for the graph.

Prim's starts with the root, then picks the minimum edge connecting a new node to the current tree until all nodes are included.

However, with the added constraints that each existing node can have at most two children.

Prim's algorithm can be adapted:

Initialize:

The tree contains just the root.

For each node not in the tree, keep track of the shortest edge to any node in the tree.

Also track for each node (except root) the shortest edge to the tree.

Additionally, for each candidate node to add, we need to ensure that when we choose an edge (u→v) (u is in the tree, v is to be connected), that the node u still has available slots.

Thus steps could be:

Initially:

- The tree includes just the root.

- All other nodes are unconnected. For each node v, track the minimal length edge from any u in the tree (connected through existing edges) such that choosing v's parent as u would be possible (u not full).

The priority queue holds possible edges (sorted by distance) that can be added without violating the children_count.

At each step:

select the minimal edge (u, v) from the current tree nodes to unconnected nodes, not already chosen.

Before adding, ensure that u has available children slots (children_count[u] <2.

Then, we can add v with parent u. Update the children_count[u].

The problem is, when multiple parents are possible for v, the next best edges might come from other nodes which were not yet connected.

Computing for each unconnected node the shortest available edge where the parent has available slots is critical.

But how to manage this:

The algorithm would be:

Initialize:

children_count for all nodes to 0, except root:

Wait root has children_count 0, available capacity 2.

parent for each node is None except root.

que = priority queue (min-heap) of edges (edge distance, u, v) where u is in the current tree, v not, and v does not have a parent yet. The edge is to connect v to u.

Wait the que needs filled initially:

For each node v (excluding root), for each node u in the current tree (just root), the edge from u to v can be considered, but only if u's children_count < 2.

Initially, root can contribute edges to any v. The minimal of those would first be chosen.

Wait, the initial current tree is just the root.

Thus, for each node v not in tree:

the edge (root, v) is available if root has not filled its capacity.

Each v will have an edge to their closest possible node in the tree (currently just root).

So the priority queue contains all edges (root → v), with the distance computed between them. Additionally, any other nodes connected to the tree.

We can populate the initial queue with all edges from root to all other nodes.

We then proceed:

initialized:

current_tree contains root.

available_nodes = all others.

Have a min-heap.

Initially, for each v not root:

the edge with the distance from v to root is the candidate.

So we push all those edges into the priority queue.

But also, note that when selecting edges from the heap, we must check whether their candidate parent can accept them.

Thus:

While the priority queue is not empty and there are nodes to add:

Extract the edge (u, v) with the smallest distance.

Check if:

- v is not already in current tree.

- u has less than two children.

If so:

add v to current tree.

Set parent[v] = u.

increment children_count[u] by 1.

Check if adding this edge has caused u to be full (now has 2 children).

Then, for all nodes w that are still unconnected (not in tree):

   for those nodes, they need a potential new edge (other edges from existing tree nodes to w).

   When a node u is full now, any edges into w via u would be now blocked.

   So, for each w, we need to find the next best parent among the available tree nodes which still have capacity and can connect to w (y higher than w).

Thus, after adding node v to the tree via parent u, we need to update the priority queue for the remaining nodes, to remove any edges to nodes that have become blocked (like via parent u now full) and to check for possibly better parents.

Wait, this is getting a bit complicated, as Prim's usually doesn't consider edge constraints.

The problem of the capacity may require, for each node w not yet in the tree, to track the best possible edge (smallest distance) that is viable.

Thus, for each candidate node w (not in the tree), track the closest possible parent that can accept it.

Thus:

Maintain a best_edge array for each node not in the tree, indicating the best (shortest distance) edge from a node in the tree which has capacity.

Initially, best_edge[v] is the edge from nearest available parent with capacity.

Whenever a parent (u) is filled (children_count reaches 2), we have to check all nodes which had that u as their best_edge and perhaps reassess.

This can be done with a priority queue, but it's a bit involved.

Perhaps, an efficient way is:

- Whenever a node u gains a new child (thus children_count increases), if it is now filled (>=2), we need to iteratively process its surrounding.

Alternatively, for each v not in the tree, we need to choose the best possible parent among the tree nodes which have remaining capacity.

So, during the algorithm, after each edge is added, the remaining unconnected nodes' best edges may have changed (because the parent's capacity changed).

To handle this, perhaps the priority queue should always contain all possible edges from the tree to unconnected nodes where the edge is valid (u's capacity is not full).

But managing this would be computationally expensive for n=400.

Let me think of how to structure the algorithm steps:

Here’s a possibility inspired by Prim's algorithm:

Initialize:

- The tree contains just the root node.

- children_count[root] =0, max capacity is 2.

Initialize a priority queue. The initial elements are all possible edges from root to every other node. Each has the distance, and the parent node is root.

The priority queue is ordered by distance.

For each other node v, we track the possible edges to them from tree nodes with capacity.

We will need to track for each node v the current best candidate (shortest distance edge where the parent has available capacity).

Then, proceed:

While there are unconnected nodes:

   Extract the edge (distance, u, v) with minimal distance from the priority queue.

   Check if V is already connected: continue (can skip since we process from the queue).

   Check if the potential parent u still has available capacity (children_count[u] <2).

   Also, check whether v is not yet connected.

   If all these conditions are true:

      Add V to the tree.

      Set parent[v] = u.

      children_count[u] +=1

      Now, check if u now has children_count =2:

         If so, then any remaining edges where the candidate parent is u are no longer considered valid.

         So, in the priority queue, edges with parent u can be ignored in the future.

      Now, we need to find new candidates for all nodes that could have been using u as their best candidate.

      (This is tricky, and without .)

Alternatively, when a node u fills up (has children_count reaches 2):

+

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct TwoSat {
    int n;
    vector<vector<int>> adj, adj_t;
    vector<int> comp, order, res;
    vector<bool> used;

    TwoSat(int _n) : n(_n), adj(2 * _n), adj_t(2 * _n), comp(2 * _n, -1), res(_n), used(2 * _n, false) {}

    void add_implication(int a, int b) {
        adj[a].push_back(b);
        adj_t[b].push_back(a);
    }

    void add_clause(int a, int b) {
        add_implication(a ^ 1, b);
        add_implication(b ^ 1, a);
    }

    void add_clause_single(int a) {
        add_implication(a ^ 1, a);
    }

    void dfs1(int u) {
        if (used[u]) return;
        used[u] = true;
        for (int v : adj[u]) dfs1(v);
        order.push_back(u);
    }

    void dfs2(int u, int c) {
        comp[u] = c;
        for (int v : adj_t[u]) 
            if (comp[v] == -1)
                dfs2(v, c);
    }

    bool solve() {
        for (int i = 0; i < 2 * n; ++i)
            if (!used[i]) dfs1(i);
        reverse(order.begin(), order.end());
        int c = 0;
        for (int u : order)
            if (comp[u] == -1)
                dfs2(u, c++);
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            if (comp[2 * i] == comp[2 * i + 1])
                return false;
            res[i] = comp[2 * i] > comp[2 * i + 1];
        }
        return true;
    }

    vector<int> get_assignment() { return res; }
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, p, M, m;
    cin >> n >> p >> M >> m;

    vector<pair<int, int>> complaints(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> complaints[i].first >> complaints[i].second;
        complaints[i].first--;
        complaints[i].second--;
    }

    vector<pair<int, int>> stations(p);
    for (int i = 0; i < p; ++i) {
        cin >> stations[i].first >> stations[i].second;
    }

    vector<pair<int, int>> interfer(m);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        cin >> interfer[i].first >> interfer[i].second;
        interfer[i].first--;
        interfer[i].second--;
    }

    vector<int> valid_f;
    for (int f = 1; f <= M; ++f) {
        bool valid = true;
        for (auto &[x, y] : complaints) {
            bool x_ok = (stations[x].first <= f && f <= stations[x].second);
            bool y_ok = (stations[y].first <= f && f <= stations[y].second);
            if (!x_ok && !y_ok) {
                valid = false;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (valid) valid_f.push_back(f);
    }

    if (valid_f.empty()) {
        cout << -1 << endl;
        return 0;
    }

    for (int f : valid_f) {
        TwoSat ts(p);

        vector<bool> allowed(p, false);
        for (int i = 0; i < p; ++i) {
            if (stations[i].first <= f && f <= stations[i].second) {
                allowed[i] = true;
            } else {
                ts.add_clause_single(2 * i + 1); // not selected
            }
        }

        bool valid_complaints = true;
        for (auto &[x, y] : complaints) {
            bool x_ok = allowed[x];
            bool y_ok = allowed[y];
            if (!x_ok && !y_ok) {
                valid_complaints = false;
                break;
            } else if (x_ok && y_ok) {
                ts.add_clause(2 * x, 2 * y);
            } else if (x_ok) {
                ts.add_clause_single(2 * x);
            } else {
                ts.add_clause_single(2 * y);
            }
        }
        if (!valid_complaints) continue;

        for (auto &[u, v] : interfer) {
            if (allowed[u] && allowed[v]) {
                ts.add_clause(2 * u + 1, 2 * v + 1);
            }
        }

        if (ts.solve()) {
            vector<int> selected;
            auto assign = ts.get_assignment();
            for (int i = 0; i < p; ++i) {
                if (assign[i]) {
                    selected.push_back(i + 1);
                }
            }
            cout << selected.size() << " " << f << "\n";
            for (int x : selected) cout << x << " ";
            cout << endl;
            return 0;
        }
    }

    cout << -1 << endl;
    return 0;

      Add V to the tree.

      Set parent[v] = u.

      children_count[u] +=1

      Now, check if u now has children_count =2:

         If so, then any remaining edges where the candidate parent is u are no longer considered valid.

      Now, we need to find new candidates for all nodes that could have been using u as their best candidate.

Alternatively, when a node u fills up (has children_count reaches 2):

   eliminate any edges from the priority queue that have u as the parent.

But how to do this efficiently?

Hmm, perhaps another approach is required. 

Perhaps the necessary approach is:

The priority queue keeps edges that are possible candidates (parent not full), and for each node v not yet in the tree, track the sorted list of possible parents (the nodes in the tree with % capacity) that are closest to it. So whenever a parent node u becomes full (or another closer option is available), we can decide to offer that option to v's choices.

This is similar to Dijkstra's algorithm, and can probably be implemented using priority queues with some deletions of possible edges. But how?

Here's a way to proceed step by step:

Maintain for each unconnected node v the current closest candidate parent that satisfies:

   (1) The parent is in the tree.

   (2) the parent still has available slots.

For each such v, we track this best (min distance) option.

The priority queue's elements are the current best options (distance, v) and a pointer to the parent.

The queue is a priority based on distance.

Initially, each v has candidates from the root (since root is the start the tree).

Thus, the initial best option for v is the edge from the root if the root can allocate to it.

The priority queue contains the best edge for each v.

Now:

   While there are nodes still without a parent:

      Extract the node v with the shortest edge (distance) among all possible current best options.

      The edge is via its chosen parent u.

      Check if the parent u still has capacity (children_count[u] <2).

          (this is crucial, because after some steps, u may have already been assigned two children and can't accept anymore)

          If no more capacity: skip this node, remove from queue, and look for next best options for v.

          — else:

              Accept this edge: assign v's parent to u.

              add this edge (v is now in the tree)

              update the children_count[u].

              

Now for this v, we are commiting to this edge. 

We now need to reevaluate other nodes' best options, especially nodes that may have considered this edge's parent (e.g. if u is now at full capacity for their parent.

But for other nodes w not in the tree: they may have another candidate parent.

This is getting a bit dicey, but maybe manage it by step:

Whenever we process node v:

"Their chosen parent is u". If u now reaches the children_count[u] ==2:

   Then u can't be a parent any more. Thus， for any other node w that had u as one of their possible candidates, we need to consider this possibility.

But we cannot know all of them upfront.

Hence, for this method:

The priority queue elements must be the current shortest path to the tree considering available parents.

Each time we assign a parent to a node v:

We must then update the availablecapacity of the parent.

Then, for any other unconnected nodes w:

   check if any of their possible current parents (nearest) is the now full u, and thus update their best option.

This update would require scanning the graph, which is not efficient for large n.

This suggests that this approach is possibly O(n²), but with a 400^2, that's 160 000.

Which might be feasible.

Alternatively, for each node w not yet connected, the best edge is re-evaluated each time.

Perhaps:

The problem is manageable in practice.

current_best[v] = (distance between v and root, root)

Add these to the priority queue.

（The priority queue stores tuples (distance, v, possible_parent).

Whenever the-best candidate changes for v, we need to update their entry in the pri。

This seems doable by：

Maintain the current best for each v, and need to update the priority queue whenever a candidate is found that is better.

时，当 a parent u becomes full (no longer can be a parent), the nodes for which the current best was via u must be forced to look for the next best option.

this is the crux of the problem.

Implementation steps:

We can represent the current best options as follows:

for each node v, store the best distance (min possible) and the best parent. 

The priority queue contains entries for the best distance of v.

But the state of the queue may be out of date because when the prefered parent becomes unavailable.

To handle this:

Each time a parent u fills its capacity (children_count[u] reaches 2):

• 所有 nodes v whose best_parent is u may need to find a new best option.

This can be done by iterating all existing v nodes and checking if they had their best candidate as u, then recompute their new best candidate:

For each这样的v:

- set their current best to the next best possible available parent (excluding u).

则:

这样, 这个 process would be O(n) per update, which could get expensive for many such updates.

However, if the maximum number of times the parent changes is limited, perhaps it will work within N^2.

Alternatively, we can use a priority queue that allows additions and deletions efficiently. This requires using a priority queue that supports decremental keys (like a Fibonacci heap), but practically in Python, this is difficult.

Perhaps a better approach is:

Initially, the priority queue holds for each v their current best option (best edge to a tree node with capacity).

We process the next shortest edge (v’s current best):

1. Check if this option is still valid:

   — the parent u still is part of the tree (~ is it, since the parent is in the tree by choice）

   yes, because only edges from the tree are considered.

   — the parent still has capacity: children_count[u] < 2.

如果 yes:

 accept this edge:

   - add the edge to the tree.

   - update children_count[u].

   —- if this caused孩子们的计数的U to reach 2, then any other nodes that have u as their current best need to lose this best option and find another one.

Because:

 when a parent reaches capacity, any other nodes relying on this parent now cannot choose them, so they must look for other options.

Thus:

We need to process these other nodes.

An efficient way to do this could be:

每当 we add a node u reaches capacity (children_count[u]=2):

 collect all nodes v whose current_best parent is u.

 For these nodes:

   重新 compute the next best candidate.

   如果 their new best is a valid edge（parent available）：

       update their current best and add to the priority queue.

   else若 there is no alternative:

       this indicates the problem is impossible (cannot form the tree).

 this requires having for each node u a list of nodes v that have chosen u as their current best. 

于是, we can:

 maintain a hash map: for each parent u, we have a list of nodes v that currently have最佳的父 is .

      我们 remove their current best entry from the priority queue (because this option is now blocked).

      We need to re-express these nodes v的 best options.

      For this, we can for each v:

        —— this requires checking all tree nodes which are above v’s y<#sep#> both ``d{{0, 1}}``
+    """
+    k0 = d0.keys() if isinstance(d0, dict) else d0

+
+TPL_FLOAT_INT = float, int
+
+# Error codes for direct compare
+DC_STAT_GOOD = 0
+"""Values are identical to FP precision, or by ``==`` operator."""
+DC_STAT_LE_LOWER = 1
+"""Values are not identical, but max diff <= lower tolerance."""
+DC_STAT_MID = 10
+"""Values differ with max difference between lower and upper tolerance."""
+DC_STAT_GE_UPPER = 100
+"""Values differ with max difference greater than or equal to upper tolerance"""  # noqa
+DC_STAT_NOT_IDENTICAL = 200
+"""Values should be identical but are not, e.g. strings or bools."""
+DC_STAT_DIFF_TYPES = 255
+"""Values are of different types"""
+DC_STAT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED = -1
+"""Direct compare is not implemented for these types"""
+DC_STAT_DIFF_SHAPES = 250
+"""Values are of different shapes."""
+
+COMPARE_STATUS_CODES = {
+    DC_STAT_GOOD: (logIdentical, True),
+    DC_STAT_LE_LOWER: (logAcceptableLow, True),
+    DC_STAT_MID: (logAcceptableHigh, True),
+    DC_STAT_NOT_IDENTICAL: (logNotIdentical, False),
+    DC_STAT_GE_UPPER: (logOutsideTols, False),
+    DC_STAT_DIFF_TYPES: (logDifferentTypes, False),
+    DC_STAT_DIFF_SHAPES: (logBadShapes, False),
+}
+"""Keys of status codes with ``(caller, return)`` values."""
+
+
+@compareDocDecorator
+def directCompare(obj0, obj1, lower, upper):
+    """
+    Return True if values are close enough to each other.
+
+    Wrapper around various comparision tests for strings, numeric, and
+    arrays.
+
+    Parameters
+    ----------
+    obj0: str or float or int or :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+    obj1: str or float or int or :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+        Objects to compare
+    {compLimits}
+    quantity: str
+        Description of the value being compared. Will be
+        used to notify the user about any differences
+
+    Returns
+    -------
+    int:
+        Status code of the comparison.
+
+        * {good} - Values are identical to floating point precision or,
+          for strings/booleans, are identical with the ``==`` operator
+        * {leLower} - Values are not identical, but the max difference
+          is less than ``lower``.
+        * {mid} - Values differ, with the max difference greater
+          than ``lower`` but less than ``upper``
+        * {geUpper} - Values differ by greater than or equal to ``upper``
+        * {notIdentical} - Values should be identical (strings, booleans),
+          but are not
+        * {diffShapes} - Numeric data has different shapes
+        * {diffTypes} - Values are of different types
+        * {notImplemented} - Type comparison is not supported. This means that
+          developers should either implement a test for this
+          data type, or use a different function
+
+    See Also
+    --------
+    * :func:`logDirectCompare` - Function that utilizes this and logs
+      the results using the :mod:`serpentTools.messages` module
+    """
+    type0 = type(obj0)
+    type1 = type(obj1)
+
+    if type0 != type1:
+        # can still compare floats and ints easily
+        if type0 not in TPL_FLOAT_INT or type1 not in TPL_FLOAT_INT:
+            return DC_STAT_DIFF_TYPES
+    if type0 in (str, bool):
+        if obj0 != obj1:
+            return DC_STAT_NOT_IDENTICAL
+        return DC_STAT_GOOD
+
+    # Convert all to numpy arrays
+    if not isinstance(obj0, Iterable):
+        obj0 = array([obj0])
+        obj1 = array([obj1])
+    else:
+        # convert to array, but return if data-type is object
+        # need some indexable structure so dicts and sets won't work
+        obj0 = array(obj0)
+        obj1 = array(obj1)
+        if obj0.dtype.name == 'object':
+            return DC_STAT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED
+    if obj0.shape != obj1.shape:
+        return DC_STAT_DIFF_SHAPES
+
+    if not upper:
+        return _directCompareIdentical(obj0, obj1)
+    return _directCompareWithTols(obj0, obj1, lower, upper)
+
+
+def _directCompareIdentical(obj0, obj1):
+    """Compare arrays that should be identical"""
+    # special case for strings
+    if obj0.dtype.name[:3] == 'str':
+        compArray = charEqual(obj0, obj1)
+    else:
+        compArray = equal(obj0, obj1)
+    if compArray.all():
+        return DC_STAT_GOOD
+    return DC_STAT_NOT_IDENTICAL
+
+
+def _directCompareWithTols(obj0, obj1, lower, upper):
+    """Compare arrays that have some allowable tolerances"""
+    diff = multiply(
+        fabs(subtract(obj0, obj1)), 100
+    )
+    nonZI = greater(fabs(obj0), LOWER_LIM_DIVISION)
+    diff[nonZI] /= obj0[nonZI]
+    maxDiff = diff.max()
+    if maxDiff < LOWER_LIM_DIVISION:
+        return DC_STAT_GOOD
+    if maxDiff <= lower:
+        return DC_STAT_LE_LOWER
+    if maxDiff >= upper:
+        return DC_STAT_GE_UPPER
+    return DC_STAT_MID
+
+
+directCompare.__doc__ = directCompare.__doc__.format(
+    good=DC_STAT_GOOD,
+    leLower=DC_STAT_LE_LOWER,
+    mid=DC_STAT_MID,
+    geUpper=DC_STAT_GE_UPPER,
+    notIdentical=DC_STAT_NOT_IDENTICAL,
+    diffTypes=DC_STAT_DIFF_TYPES,
+    notImplemented=DC_STAT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED,
+    diffShapes=DC_STAT_DIFF_SHAPES,
+)
+
+
+@compareDocDecorator
+def logDirectCompare(obj0, obj1, lower, upper, quantity):
+    """
+    Compare objects using :func:`directCompare` and log the result
+
+    Parameters
+    ----------
+    obj0: str or float or int or :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+    obj1: str or float or int or :class:`numpy.ndarray`
+        Objects to compare
+    {compLimits}
+    quantity: str
+        Description of the value being compared. Will be
+        used to notify the user about any differences
+
+    Returns
+    -------
+    bool:
+        ``True`` if the objects agree according to tolerances, or numerics
+        differ less than ``upper``. ``False`` otherwise
+
+    Raises
+    ------
+    TypeError:
+        If the objects being compared are not supported by
+        :func:`directCompare`.  Developers should either extend the
+        function or utilize a different comparison function
+
+    See Also
+    --------
+    * :func:`directCompare` - function that does the comparison
+    * :func:`getOverlaps` - function for evaluating values with uncertainties
+    * :func:`getLogOverlaps` - function that logs the result of stastistical
+      comparisions
+    """
+    result = directCompare(obj0, obj1, lower, upper)
+    if result < 0:  # failures
+        if result == DC_STAT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED:
+            raise TypeError(
+                "directCompare is not configured to make tests on objects "
+                "of type {tp}\n\tQuantity: {k}\n\tUsers: Create a issue on "
+                "GitHub to alert developers<#sep#>0.008 = 6.528 in Ans. ______________________________________________________________________________ 1-15 V = xyz, and x = a   a, y = b   b, z = c   c, V abc    V a a b b c c abc bc a ac b ab c a b c b c a c a b a b c                            The higher order terms in  are negligible.
