#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Solution {
    bool found = false;
    string j1, j2;
    pair<int, int> sq1, sq2;
};

vector<string> generate_pack() {
    vector<string> ranks = {"2", "3", "4", "5", "6", "7", "8", "9", "T", "J", "Q", "K", "A"};
    vector<string> suits = {"C", "D", "H", "S"};
    vector<string> pack;
    for (const auto& r : ranks) {
        for (const auto& s : suits) {
            pack.push_back(r + s);
        }
    }
    pack.push_back("J1");
    pack.push_back("J2");
    return pack;
}

bool is_valid_square(const vector<vector<string>>& grid, int r, int c) {
    char suit = grid[r][c][1];
    bool same = true;
    set<char> ranks;
    for (int i = r; i < r+3; ++i) {
        for (int j = c; j < c+3; ++j) {
            const string& card = grid[i][j];
            if (card[1] != suit) same = false;
            ranks.insert(card[0]);
        }
    }
    return same || ranks.size() == 9;
}

bool no_overlap(int r1, int c1, int r2, int c2) {
    return (r1+3 <= r2) || (r2+3 <= r1) || (c1+3 <= c2) || (c2+3 <= c1);
}

Solution check(const vector<vector<string>>& grid, int n, int m) {
    vector<pair<int, int>> valid;
    for (int r = 0; r <= n-3; ++r) {
        for (int c = 0; c <= m-3; ++c) {
            if (is_valid_square(grid, r, c)) {
                valid.emplace_back(r, c);
            }
        }
    }
    for (size_t i = 0; i < valid.size(); ++i) {
        for (size_t j = i+1; j < valid.size(); ++j) {
            auto [r1, c1] = valid[i];
            auto [r2, c2] = valid[j];
            if (no_overlap(r1, c1, r2, c2)) {
                return {true, "", "", {r1, c1}, {r2, c2}};
            }
        }
    }
    return {false};
}

Solution solve(const vector<vector<string>>& orig, int n, int m, const vector<pair<int, int>>& j1, const vector<pair<int, int>>& j2, const vector<string>& avail) {
    int jcnt = j1.size() + j2.size();
    if (jcnt == 0) {
        return check(orig, n, m);
    } else if (jcnt == 1) {
        for (const string& x : avail) {
            auto grid = orig;
            if (j1.size()) grid[j1[0].first][j1[0].second] = x;
            else grid[j2[0].first][j2[0].second] = x;
            auto res = check(grid, n, m);
            if (res.found) {
                res.j1 = j1.size() ? x : "";
                res.j2 = j2.size() ? x : "";
                return res;
            }
        }
    } else {
        for (int i = 0; i < avail.size(); ++i) {
            for (int j = 0; j < avail.size(); ++j) {
                if (i == j) continue;
                auto grid = orig;
                grid[j1[0].first][j1[0].second] = avail[i];
                grid[j2[0].first][j2[0].second] = avail[j];
                auto res = check(grid, n, m);
                if (res.found) {
                    res.j1 = avail[i];
                    res.j2 = avail[j];
                    return res;
                }
            }
        }
    }
    return {false};
}

int main() {
    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    vector<vector<string>> grid(n, vector<string>(m));
    set<string> present;
    vector<pair<int, int>> j1_pos, j2_pos;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < m; ++j) {
            cin >> grid[i][j];
            string& c = grid[i][j];
            present.insert(c);
            if (c == "J1") j1_pos.emplace_back(i, j);
            else if (c == "J2") j2_pos.emplace_back(i, j);
        }
    }
    vector<string> avail;
    for (const auto& c : generate_pack()) {
        if (!present.count(c)) avail.push_back(c);
    }
    Solution sol = solve(grid, n, m, j1_pos, j2_pos, avail);
    if (!sol.found) {
        cout << "No solution.\n";
        return 0;
    }
    cout << "Solution exists.\n";
    if (j1_pos.empty() && j2_pos.empty()) {
        cout << "There are no jokers.\n";
    } else if (j1_pos.size() + j2_pos.size() == 1) {
        cout << "Replace J" << (j1_pos.size() ? "1" : "2") << " with " << sol.j1 << ".\n";
    } else {
        cout << "Replace J1 with " << sol.j1 << " and J2 with " << sol.j2 << ".\n";
    }
    auto [r1, c1] = sol.sq1;
    auto [r2, c2] = sol.sq2;
    cout << "Put the first square to (" << r1+1 << ", " << c1+1 << ").\n";
    cout << "Put the second square to (" << r2+1 << ", " << c2+1 << ").\n";
    return 0;

**Final Answer**
The real parameter \( m \) must satisfy \( \boxed{[15 - 6\sqrt{6}, 49 + 10\sqrt{21}]} \).
</think>To find all real numbers \( m \) such that the system of equations has real solutions, we start by analyzing the given equations:

1. \( x^2 + 3xz + z^2 = 1 \)
2. \( 3y^2 + 3yz + z^2 = 4 \)
3. \( x^2 - xy + y^2 = m \)

First, we note that the first two equations are quadratic in \( x \) and \( y \) respectively. For real solutions, the discriminants of these quadratics must be non-negative. For the first equation, the discriminant is always positive, ensuring real solutions for \( x \) for any \( z \). For the second equation, the discriminant gives the constraint \( |z| \leq 4 \).

Next, we express \( x \) and \( y \) in terms of \( z \) using the quadratic formula. For \( x \):
\[ x = \frac{-3z \pm \sqrt{5z^2 + 4}}{2} \]

For \( y \):
\[ y = \frac{-3z \pm \sqrt{48 - 3z^2}}{6} \]

Substituting these expressions into the third equation \( x^2 - xy + y^2 = m \) and simplifying, we find that \( m \) depends on \( z \) and the signs chosen in the solutions for \( x \) and \( y \).

To find the range of \( m \), we analyze specific values of \( z \):

1. **When \( z = 2 \):**
   - From the first equation: \( x^2 + 6x + 4 = 1 \Rightarrow x^2 + 6x + 3 = 0 \Rightarrow x = -3 \pm \sqrt{6} \)
   - From the second equation: \( 3y^2 + 6y + 4 = 4 \Rightarrow 3y^2 + 6y = 0 \Rightarrow y = 0 \text{ or } y = -2 \)
   - For \( x = -3 + \sqrt{6} \) and \( y = 0 \):
     \[ m = (-3 + \sqrt{6})^2 = 9 - 6\sqrt{6} + 6 = 15 - 6\sqrt{6} \]
   - For \( x = -3 - \sqrt{6} \) and \( y = 0 \):
     \[ m = (-3 - \sqrt{6})^2 = 9 + 6\sqrt{6} + 6 = 15 + 6\sqrt{6} \]

2. **When \( z = 4 \):**
   - From the first equation: \( x^2 + 12x + 16 = 1 \Rightarrow x^2 + 12x + 15 = 0 \Rightarrow x = -6 \pm \sqrt{21} \)
   - From the second equation: \( 3y^2 + 12y + 16 = 4 \Rightarrow 3y^2 + 12y + 12 = 0 \Rightarrow y = -2 \)
   - For \( x = -6 - \sqrt{21} \) and \( y = -2 \):

每当 a parent u becomes full (children_count reaches 2):

   for all v in u的 candidates 的 list:

      我们 remove their current best entry from the priority queue (because this option is now blocked).

      We need to re-express these nodes v的 best options.

      For this, we can for each v:

          iterate through all nodes in the current tree that can be a parent of v (i.e., have a higher y-coordinate than v AND have capacity <2 )

Suppose we have for each node v:

 whenever its best option is invalidated:

   we can recompute the current best by checking all possible nodes in the tree (excluding those full) and find the closest father.

To compute the new best for v:

遍历 all nodes in the tree (except罗特) who are not full (children_count <2), and find the one closest to v with (their y is higher than v's)

等效 to searching the current tree nodes with available capacity and higher y, and find the closest to $$ v.

这 would be O (k) per node, where k is the number of tree nodes, which could be O(N^2) in worst case.

Alternatively, to optimize:

 maintain a list of eligible parents (油) for the current tree nodes that can still accept children (children_count[u]<2).

每当 a new edge is added (导致 u reaches capacity)：这样父油的 eligibility removed from the list.

そして:

 for any nodes需要重新 compute their bester, they iterate过 this list of eligible parents and find the nearest.

这也可能 be feasible.

Thus, a rough plan would be：

数据结构:

— eligible_parents: a list of node indices where the node has capacity (children_count <2) and has been added to the tree (parent is in the tree).

— for each node v, its current best distance and candidate parent are stored.

The priority queue contains entries corresponding to当前 nodes not in the tree and their best distance.

步骤:

Initialize:

current_tree set has root.

所有 nodes except root have their current best parent set to root, provided root has capacity.

addTo eligible_parents the root。

priorityQueue is initialized with所有 edges from root to other nodes的距离.

While the priority queue is non-empty and there are unfinished nodes:

   extract the smallest distance entry (distance, v 的节点, candidate_parent.

   Check if candidate_parent is still eligible:

      — is the candidate parent in the eligible_parents?

      — or, is the node's current best still pointing to this candidate?

     (可能存在 other changes that have invalidated this entry since the queue was added).

因此：

首先 check if the candidate parent is still in eligible_parents (i.e not full),并且 the distance is the same as the current distance for v.

或者, if the current_b est for v is no longer this option.

如果 this option is invalidated:

则, continue to next element from the queue.

， otherwise proceed:

accept this edge:

将 v added to current_tree.

Set its parent as the candidate parent.

Update the child count of candidate parent 上.

如果 the candidate parent reaches 2的 children_count:

       remove them from eligible_parents.

       then, iterate throat all nodes (not in the tree) wht were possibly depending on this parent's candidacy.

For these nodes:

       they need to recompute their best_parent.

      To compute their new best_parent:

          Iterate all eligible_parents 找到 the one with最小 distance to v.

          计算 the smallest distance to any eligible parent.

          如果 there's none, the problem is impossible.

          设置 their new best parent and push this new entry to the priority queue.

          also, remove the old entry form the queue (if possible).

这 requires efficiently managing the priority queue and entries.

然而，在 practice, this might be cumbersome.

An alternative idea is to ignore the queue and just always recompute the best edge for nodes when they are considered.

Perhaps the following approach could be:

每次，选择 next unconnected node 的 closest 与 some eligible parent:

Keep track of all eligible_parents.

Loop while more nodes are needed:

   find the closest edge between any eligible parent and an unconnected node.

这:

   nested loop over all eligible parents and their possible edges to unconnected nodes.

 Finding the minimal edge among all these would take O(k*m), where k is the number of eligible parents and m是未存的节点数.

还可能 expensive.

 Hmm. Perhaps it's better to proceed with the greedy approach as initially described, but with the modification that when considering edges, the parent must already be in the tree.

The issue in my initial example was that when considering the edge A-B (parent A, child B), the parent node A must've already been connected to the root.

So, the edge can only be processed if the parent node U has already been connected themselves.

Hence, in the sorting of edges, edges involving unconnected parental nodes should be considered only after those parents are themselves connected.

How to enforce that?

The times edges can be considered are in reverse ordering of their parental's connection.

This is quite complicated.

Alternatively, think of the problem as building the tree level by level, where each level is determined by its y-coordinate.

Since the nodes are processed in decreasing order of y:

Once all nodes with higher y are processed (i.e their children are chosen), then we can proceed to connect lower nodes.

This would ensure that parents are added before children.

Thus, the following approach:

1. sort all nodes by y in decreasing order.

 thus顺序为 v0 (root), v1, v2, ..., vn-1.

2. iterate through the nodes in increasing order (starting with v0, then v1,....)

Each node processing is done in the order of y increasing, so by the time we reach vi, all nodes with higher y (preceding nodes) are already added and their connections are processed.

 For each vi (starting from v1) :

 The node has to choose a parent from the preceding nodes (before it in the queue) which can still accept it (parent's children_count <2 and haven't done so.

 Among all candidate parents, choose the one with the smallest distance (to minimize total length).

 This way, for each node vi, its parent must be chosen from already connected nodes (which are all the nodes with higher y), and the paren has available capacity.

 This Approach can be implemented as:

Proceed as follows:

 sorted_nodes = sorted by y坐标 decreasing.

 the root is node0.

Initialize:

   parents array set to None for all, except root has no parent.

 children counts all zero except root's is :

   ( initially zero)

 proceed:

 for each node in the list from node1 to last:

   current_node = node_i.

   find the closest parent node among all preceding node (那些 are in前面's indices) which have children_count <2:

   the closest is the one with minimum distance.

   노特 such parent exists ^ none可以:

      return -1

   else:

      choose the closest such, the closest?

 This approach ensures that we choose the nearest possible parent available among 高у的 nodes.

 This is immediately plausible because each node is considered in order of decreasing y-coordinate, and thus you can only pick a parent from earlier (thus, higher y) already processed nodes.

 这对es ensure that parents are in the tree (since their own connections have been processed already).

 Thus, this approach may work.

 Thus, the algorithm would:

- sort the nodes in decreasing order of y.

 - Then, for each node after the root (nodes 1~n-1):

   - find among all prior nodes (they have higher y), which still have capacity, the closest one.

   - select the closest such node as the parent, and record this edge.

   - this way the parent is existing and available.

- The total distance is the sum of the chosen edges.

- If any node cannot find a parent (no candidate with capacity), 返回-1.

 This approach seems valid and可能 correct.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

vector<vector<int>> adj, adj_t;
vector<bool> visited;
vector<int> order;

void dfs1(int u) {
    stack<pair<int, bool>> st;
    st.push({u, false});
    while (!st.empty()) {
        auto [v, processed] = st.top();
        st.pop();
        if (processed) {
            order.push_back(v);
            continue;
        }
        if (visited[v]) continue;
        visited[v] = true;
        st.push({v, true});
        for (int w : adj[v]) {
            if (!visited[w]) {
                st.push({w, false});
            }
        }
    }
}

vector<vector<int>> kosaraju(int n) {
    visited.assign(n + 1, false);
    order.clear();
    for (int u = 1; u <= n; ++u) {
        if (!visited[u]) {
            dfs1(u);
        }
    }
    reverse(order.begin(), order.end());
    vector<vector<int>> components;
    visited.assign(n + 1, false);
    for (int u : order) {
        if (!visited[u]) {
            stack<int> st;
            st.push(u);
            visited[u] = true;
            vector<int> component;
            while (!st.empty()) {
                int v = st.top();
                st.pop();
                component.push_back(v);
                for (int w : adj_t[v]) {
                    if (!visited[w]) {
                        visited[w] = true;
                        st.push(w);
                    }
                }
            }
            components.push_back(component);
        }
    }
    return components;
}

void solve() {
    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;
    adj.assign(n + 1, vector<int>());
    adj_t.assign(n + 1, vector<int>());
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int a, b;
        cin >> a >> b;
        adj[a].push_back(b);
        adj_t[b].push_back(a);
    }
    vector<vector<int>> components = kosaraju(n);
    if (components.empty()) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return;
    }
    vector<int> jury_candidate = components[0];
    int k = jury_candidate.size();
    if (k >= 1 && k <= n - 1) {
        vector<bool> in_jury(n + 1, false);
        for (int u : jury_candidate) {
            in_jury[u] = true;
        }
        vector<int> contestants;
        for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
            if (!in_jury[i]) {
                contestants.push_back(i);
            }
        }
        cout << "Yes\n";
        cout << k << " " << n - k << "\n";
        for (int u : jury_candidate) {
            cout << u << " ";
        }
        cout << "\n";
        for (int u : contestants) {
            cout << u << " ";
        }
        cout << "\n";
    } else {
        cout << "No\n";
    }
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);
    int t;
    cin >> t;
    while (t--) {
        solve();
        if (t > 0) {
            string empty_line;
            getline(cin, empty_line);
        }
    }
    return 0;

   - For \( x = -6 - \sqrt{21} \) and \( y = -2 \):
     \[ m = (-6 - \sqrt{21})^2 + 2(-6 - \sqrt{21}) + 4 = 36 + 12\sqrt{21} + 21 - 12 - 2\sqrt{21} + 4 = 49 + 10\sqrt{21} \]

     \[ m = (-6 + \sqrt{21})^2 + 2(-6 + \sqrt{21}) + 4 = 36 - 12\sqrt{21} + 21 - 12 + 2\sqrt{21} + 4 = 49 - 10\sqrt{21} \]

Therefore, the range of \( m \) is all real numbers between these two values. Hence, the solution is:

\[

## Summary of issue
When restricting the scope of the variables extracted from the results file to only group constants, the reader fails. 

It is also worth noting that, when non-group constant data are requested as well, 'ABS_KEFF' and 'INF_FLX', for example, the reader will not fail, but the universes produced will not have group constant data

## Code for reproducing the issue
```
import serpentTools
serpentTools.settings.rc['xs.variableExtras'] = ['INF_FLX', ] 
serpentTools.readDataFile('pwr_res.m')
```
Alternative case

r = serpentTools.readDataFile('pwr_res.m')
u = r.getUniv('0', index=1)
u.infExp
# returns {}
```

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;
using ll = long long;

struct Edge {
    int a, b;
    ll l;
};

ll gcd(ll a, ll b) {
    a = abs(a);
    b = abs(b);
    while (b) {
        a %= b;
        swap(a, b);
    }
    return a;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    vector<Edge> edges(m);
    vector<vector<int>> adj(n+1), adj_t(n+1);

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int a, b;
        ll l;
        cin >> a >> b >> l;
        edges[i] = {a, b, l};
        adj[a].push_back(b);
        adj_t[b].push_back(a);
    }

    // Kosaraju's algorithm to find SCCs
    vector<int> order;
    vector<bool> visited(n+1, false);

    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (!visited[i]) {
            stack<int> st;
            st.push(i);
            visited[i] = true;
            while (!st.empty()) {
                int u = st.top();
                bool found = false;
                for (int v : adj[u]) {
                    if (!visited[v]) {
                        visited[v] = true;
                        st.push(v);
                        found = true;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (!found) {
                    st.pop();
                    order.push_back(u);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    reverse(order.begin(), order.end());
    fill(visited.begin(), visited.end(), false);
    vector<int> component(n+1, -1);
    int current_component = 0;
    vector<vector<int>> components;

    for (int u : order) {
        if (!visited[u]) {
            stack<int> st;
            st.push(u);
            visited[u] = true;
            component[u] = current_component;
            vector<int> current_nodes;
            current_nodes.push_back(u);
            while (!st.empty()) {
                int v = st.top();
                st.pop();
                for (int w : adj_t[v]) {
                    if (!visited[w]) {
                        visited[w] = true;
                        component[w] = current_component;
                        st.push(w);
                        current_nodes.push_back(w);
                    }
                }
            }
            components.push_back(current_nodes);
            current_component++;
        }
    }

    // Build component edges
    vector<vector<Edge>> component_edges(current_component);
    for (auto& e : edges) {
        int a = e.a, b = e.b;
        if (component[a] == component[b]) {
            component_edges[component[a]].push_back(e);
        }
    }

    // Compute cycle_gcd for each component
    vector<ll> cycle_gcd(current_component, 0);

    for (int c = 0; c < current_component; ++c) {
        const auto& comp_edges = component_edges[c];
        if (comp_edges.empty()) {
            cycle_gcd[c] = 0;
            continue;
        }

        unordered_map<int, vector<pair<int, ll>>> adj_comp;
        for (const auto& e : comp_edges) {
            adj_comp[e.a].emplace_back(e.b, e.l);
        }

        const auto& nodes = components[c];
        if (nodes.empty()) {
            cycle_gcd[c] = 0;
            continue;
        }

        int root = nodes[0];
        unordered_map<int, ll> d;
        d[root] = 0;
        queue<int> q;
        q.push(root);
        ll g = 0;

        while (!q.empty()) {
            int u = q.front();
            q.pop();

            if (adj_comp.find(u) == adj_comp.end()) continue;

            for (const auto& edge : adj_comp[u]) {
                int v = edge.first;
                ll l = edge.second;

                if (d.find(v) == d.end()) {
                    d[v] = d[u] + l;
                    q.push(v);
                } else {
                    ll candidate = d[u] + l - d[v];
                    g = gcd(g, candidate);
                }
            }
        }

        cycle_gcd[c] = g;
    }

    // Process queries
    int q;
    cin >> q;
    while (q--) {
        int v, s, t;
        cin >> v >> s >> t;

        if (s % t == 0) {
            cout << "YES\n";
            continue;
        }

        int c = component[v];
        ll g = cycle_gcd[c];

        if (g == 0) {
            cout << "NO\n";
            continue;
        }

        ll d = gcd(g, t);
        if (s % d == 0) {
            cout << "YES\n";
        } else {
            cout << "NO\n";
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>Solve the equation \(\sqrt{4 - \sqrt{4 - x}} = x\) for \(x > 0\).

<think>
Okay, so I need to solve the equation √(4 - √(4 - x)) = x for x > 0. Hmm, let's see. Radical equations can be tricky, but usually squaring both sides helps. Let me start by doing that.

First, the equation is √(4 - √(4 - x)) = x. If I square both sides, I can eliminate the outer square root. Let me write that down:

[√(4 - √(4 - x))]² = x²  
So that simplifies to:  
4 - √(4 - x) = x²

Alright, now we have 4 minus another square root equals x squared. Let me rearrange this to isolate the remaining square root. Subtract 4 from both sides? Wait, actually, maybe move the square root to the other side. Let's see:

4 - x² = √(4 - x)

Yes, that looks better. Now, the equation is 4 - x² equals the square root of (4 - x). To get rid of this square root, I can square both sides again. Let's do that.

(4 - x²)² = [√(4 - x)]²  
Which simplifies to:  
(4 - x²)² = 4 - x

Expanding the left side. Let's compute (4 - x²)². Remember, (a - b)² = a² - 2ab + b². So:

4² - 2*4*x² + (x²)² = 4 - x  
16 - 8x² + x⁴ = 4 - x

Now, bring all terms to one side to set the equation to zero. Subtract 4 and add x to both sides:

16 - 8x² + x⁴ - 4 + x = 0  
Simplify 16 - 4 to 12:  
12 + x - 8x² + x⁴ = 0

Let me rearrange the terms in descending order of degree:

x⁴ - 8x² + x + 12 = 0

Hmm, so now we have a quartic (fourth-degree) equation. Quartic equations can be tough, but maybe this factors nicely. Let me try to factor it.

First, perhaps we can factor by grouping or look for rational roots using the Rational Root Theorem. The possible rational roots are factors of 12 divided by factors of 1, so ±1, ±2, ±3, ±4, ±6, ±12.

Let's test x = 1:

1⁴ - 8*1² + 1 + 12 = 1 - 8 + 1 + 12 = 6 ≠ 0

x = -1:

(-1)^4 - 8*(-1)^2 + (-1) + 12 = 1 - 8 -1 +12 = 4 ≠ 0

x = 2:

16 - 32 + 2 +12 = (16 + 2 +12) -32 = 30 -32 = -2 ≠ 0

x = -2:

16 - 32 + (-2) +12 = same as above but with -2: 16 -32= -16; -16 -2= -18; -18 +12= -6 ≠0

x = 3:

81 - 8*9 + 3 +12 = 81 -72 +15 = 24 ≠0

x = -3:

81 - 72 -3 +12= (81 -72)=9; 9 -3 +12=18 ≠0

x=4:

256 - 8*16 +4 +12 =256 -128 +16= 144≠0

x= -4:

256 - 128 -4 +12=136≠0

x=6: Probably too big, but test if necessary.

Wait, none of the integer roots are working. Maybe this quartic doesn't factor nicely with integer roots. Maybe there's a quadratic factor?

Alternatively, since this quartic equation came from squaring twice, maybe some solutions are extraneous. So even if we find solutions, we have to check them in the original equation.

Alternatively, let's think if there's a substitution we can do. Let me let y = x². But the equation is x⁴ -8x² +x +12=0. Maybe that's not helpful. Alternatively, since there's a linear term x, substitution might complicate. Let's see:

Alternatively, maybe try factoring as (x² + ax + b)(x² + cx + d). Let's attempt that.

Suppose the quartic factors into two quadratics:

(x² + ax + b)(x² + cx + d) = x⁴ + (a + c)x³ + (ac + b + d)x² + (ad + bc)x + bd

Comparing to our equation x⁴ -8x² +x +12, we can set up equations:

1. Coefficient of x³: a + c = 0 (since there's no x³ term in the quartic)
2. Coefficient of x²: ac + b + d = -8
3. Coefficient of x: ad + bc = 1
4. Constant term: bd = 12

From equation 1: c = -a

Plugging c = -a into equation 2:

a*(-a) + b + d = -8 => -a² + b + d = -8

Equation 3: a*d + b*(-a) = a(d - b) = 1

Equation 4: b*d =12

Hmm. So we need to find integers a, b, d such that:

- a² = b + d +8

- a(d - b) =1

- b*d=12

Since a(d - b) =1 and a must be integer, possible a is ±1.

Let’s try a=1:

Then from equation 3: 1*(d - b) =1 => d - b =1 => d = b +1

From equation 4: b*(b +1) =12. Let's solve this:

b² + b -12=0

Discriminant: 1 +48=49. Solutions: b=(-1±7)/2. So b=(6)/2=3 or b=(-8)/2=-4.

Case 1: b=3. Then d=4.

From equation 2: -1² +3 +4= -1 +7=6 ≠-8. Doesn't work.

Case 2: b=-4. Then d=-3.

From equation 2: -1² + (-4) + (-3)= -1 -7= -8. Which matches. So this works.

Thus, a=1, c=-1, b=-4, d=-3.

Therefore, the quartic factors as (x² + x -4)(x² -x -3)

Let me check this multiplication:

(x² + x -4)(x² -x -3) = x^4 -x³ -3x² + x³ -x² -3x -4x² +4x +12

Combine like terms:

x^4 + ( -x³ + x³ ) + (-3x² -x² -4x²) + (-3x +4x ) +12  
Simplifies to:

x⁴ + 0x³ -8x² + x +12. Which matches! So the quartic factors into (x² +x -4)(x² -x -3) =0.

So the solutions are the roots of each quadratic:

First quadratic: x² +x -4=0. Solutions:

x = [-1 ±√(1 +16)]/2 = [-1 ±√17]/2

Second quadratic: x² -x -3=0. Solutions:

x = [1 ±√(1 +12)]/2 = [1 ±√13]/2

Therefore, the quartic equation has four roots: (-1 +√17)/2, (-1 -√17)/2, (1 +√13)/2, (1 -√13)/2.

But the original problem states x >0, so we can discard negative roots.

Let's evaluate each possible root:

First quadratic: x = [-1 +√17]/2 ≈ (-1 +4.123)/2≈3.123/2≈1.5615 (positive)
x = [-1 -√17]/2 which is negative, so discard.

Second quadratic: x = [1 +√13]/2≈(1 +3.605)/2≈4.605/2≈2.30<#sep#>human: For his computer science class, Jacob builds a model tree with sticks and balls containing n nodes in the shape of a tree. Jacob has spent ai minutes building the i-th ball in the tree.

Jacob's teacher will evaluate his model and grade Jacob based on the effort he has put in. However, she does not have enough time to search his whole tree to determine this; Jacob knows that she will examine the first k nodes in a DFS-order traversal of the tree. She will then assign Jacob a grade equal to the minimum ai she finds among those k nodes.

Though Jacob does not have enough time to rebuild his model, he can choose the root node that his teacher starts from. Furthermore, he can rearrange the list of neighbors of each node in any order he likes. Help Jacob find the best grade he can get on this assignment.

A DFS-order traversal is an ordering of the nodes of a rooted tree, built by a recursive DFS-procedure initially called on the root of the tree. When called on a given node v, the procedure does the following: 

  1. Print v. 
  2. Traverse the list of neighbors of the node v in order and iteratively call DFS-procedure on each one. Do not call DFS-procedure on node u if you came to node v directly from u.
Input

The first line of the input contains two positive integers, n and k (2 ≤ n ≤ 200 000, 1 ≤ k ≤ n) — the number of balls in Jacob's tree and the number of balls the teacher will inspect.

The second line contains n integers, ai (1 ≤ ai ≤ 1 000 000), the time Jacob used to build the i-th ball.

Each of the next n - 1 lines contains two integers ui, vi (1 ≤ ui, vi ≤ n, ui ≠ vi) representing a connection in Jacob's tree between balls ui and vi.

Output

Print a single integer — the maximum grade Jacob can get by picking the right root of the tree and rearranging the list of neighbors.

Examples

Input

5 3
3 6 1 4 2
1 2
2 4
2 5
1 3


Output

3


Input

4 2
1 5 5 5
1 2
1 3
1 4


Output

1

Note

In the first sample, Jacob can root the tree at node 2 and order 2's neighbors in the order 4, 1, 5 (all other nodes have at most two neighbors). The resulting preorder traversal is 2, 4, 1, 3, 5, and the minimum ai of the first 3 nodes is 3.

In the second sample, it is clear that any preorder traversal will contain node 1 as either its first or second node, so Jacob cannot do better than a grade of 1.

The root is part of the path, then its children and their paths.

## Summary of issue
When reading a file with uncertainties, and the `branching.areUncsPresent` setting is `True`, the code
errors.
Also, I think we can eliminate this setting entirely, because when a file has uncertainties,  the value N still indicates the number of groups/values present without uncertainties.
https://github.com/CORE-GATECH-GROUP/serpent-tools/blob/0e61685f19f644b9bb7a1c2a2b3d8aec30ffcaf5/serpentTools/data/demo.coe#L6-L13
From the [SERPENT wiki](http://serpent.vtt.fi/mediawiki/index.php/Automated_burnup_sequence#Output):
```
The relative statistical errors are not printed by default, but they can be included with an option in the 
set coefpara card, which also defines list of parameters included in the output. In such case the data is 
provided in mean value - statistical error pairs and N gives the number of pairs.
```
 
## Code for reproducing the issue
```
>>> import serpentTools
>>> serpentTools.settings.rc['branching.areUncsPresent'] = True
>>> serpentTools.readDataFile('demo.coe')
```

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Box {
    int id;
    int original_size;
    int current_size;
    int target;
    unordered_set<int> presents;
    Box() {}
    Box(int id, const vector<int>& p) : id(id), original_size(p.size()), current_size(p.size()) {
        for (int x : p) presents.insert(x);
    }
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    vector<Box> boxes;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        int s;
        cin >> s;
        vector<int> p(s);
        for (int& x : p) cin >> x;
        boxes.emplace_back(i+1, p);
    }

    int S = 0;
    for (auto& box : boxes) S += box.original_size;
    int t = S / n;
    int r = S % n;

    vector<int> indices(n);
    iota(indices.begin(), indices.end(), 0);
    sort(indices.begin(), indices.end(), [&](int a, int b) {
        return boxes[a].original_size > boxes[b].original_size;
    });

    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (i < r) boxes[indices[i]].target = t + 1;
        else boxes[indices[i]].target = t;
    }

    deque<Box*> active_deficit;
    for (auto& box : boxes) {
        if (box.current_size < box.target) {
            active_deficit.push_back(&box);
        }
    }

    vector<tuple<int, int, int>> moves;

    for (auto& box : boxes) {
        if (box.current_size <= box.target) continue;

        vector<int> presents_list;
        for (int x : box.presents) presents_list.push_back(x);
        int idx = 0;

        while (box.current_size > box.target && idx < (int)presents_list.size()) {
            int x = presents_list[idx++];
            if (!box.presents.count(x)) continue;

            while (!active_deficit.empty()) {
                Box* d = active_deficit.front();
                active_deficit.pop_front();

                if (d->current_size >= d->target) continue;

                if (d->presents.count(x)) {
                    active_deficit.push_back(d);
                    continue;
                }

                box.presents.erase(x);
                d->presents.insert(x);
                moves.emplace_back(box.id, d->id, x);
                box.current_size--;
                d->current_size++;

                if (d->current_size < d->target) {
                    active_deficit.push_back(d);
                }
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    cout << moves.size() << '\n';
    for (auto& [from, to, kind] : moves) {
        cout << from << ' ' << to << ' ' << kind << '\n';
    }

    return 0;

But the original problem states x >0, so we can discard negative roots.

Let's evaluate each possible root:

First quadratic: x = [-1 +√17]/2 ≈ (-1 +4.123)/2≈3.123/2≈1.5615 (positive)
x = [-1 -√17]/2 which is negative, so discard.

Second quadratic: x = [1 +√13]/2≈(1 +3.605)/2≈4.605/2≈2.3025 (positive)
x = [1 -√13]/2≈(1 -3.605)/2≈-2.605/2≈-1.3025 (negative, discard)

So possible solutions are x ≈1.5615 and x≈2.3025.

But since we squared the equation twice, we need to check these solutions in the original equation.

Original equation: √(4 - √(4 - x)) = x

Check x ≈1.5615:

Compute inner square root: √(4 - x) ≈√(4 -1.5615)=√(2.4385)≈1.5615

Then compute outer expression: 4 -1.5615≈2.4385, then √(2.4385)≈1.5615, which equals x. So this seems to check out.

Check x ≈2.3025:

Compute inner square root: √(4 -2.3025)=√(1.6975)≈1.303

Then compute outer expression:4 -1.303≈2.697, then √(2.697)≈1.642, which is not equal to x≈2.3025. So this is an extraneous solution.

Therefore, the only valid solution is x = (-1 +√17)/2.

Wait, let me verify that again.

Wait, when I checked x≈1.5615 (which is (√17 -1)/2≈(4.123 -1)/2≈3.123/2≈1.5615), the computation was:

Inner sqrt(4 -1.5615)=sqrt(2.4385)= approx 1.5615, then 4 -1.5615=2.4385, sqrt(2.4385)= approx1.5615, which equals x. So that works.

But when I tried x≈2.3025 (which is (1 +√13)/2≈(1 +3.605)/2≈2.3025), inner sqrt(4 -2.3025)=sqrt(1.6975)=approx1.303, then 4 -1.303=2.697, sqrt of that is approx1.642, which is not equal to 2.3025, so that's extraneous.

Therefore, only x=(√17 -1)/2 is the solution.

But let me check with exact values for more precision.

Let me compute (sqrt(17)-1)/2:

sqrt(17) is about 4.1231, so subtract 1: 3.1231, divide by 2: ≈1.5616.

Now, plug into original equation:

Left side: sqrt(4 - sqrt(4 - x)).

Compute 4 - x = 4 -1.5616≈2.4384. sqrt of that is≈1.5616. Then 4 -1.5616≈2.4384, sqrt of that is≈1.5616, which equals x. Perfect.

For the other root, (1 + sqrt(13))/2≈(1 +3.6055)/2≈2.3027. Let's compute the left side:

4 - x≈4 -2.3027≈1.6973. sqrt≈1.3027. Then 4 -1.3027≈2.6973. sqrt≈1.6424, which is not equal to 2.3027.

Thus, the only solution is x=(sqrt(17)-1)/2.

But let me check if there are other possible solutions. For example, maybe x=0? But the problem states x>0. What about x=2? Let's check x=2:

Left side: sqrt(4 - sqrt(4 -2))=sqrt(4 -sqrt(2))≈sqrt(4 -1.414)=sqrt(2.586)≈1.608, which is not 2. So x=2 is not a solution.

Another test: x=1. Let's compute left side:

sqrt(4 - sqrt(4 -1))=sqrt(4 -sqrt(3))≈sqrt(4 -1.732)=sqrt(2.268)≈1.506, which is not 1.

Therefore, the only solution is x=(sqrt(17)-1)/2. Let me write this in exact terms.

√17 is approximately 4.1231, so (√17 -1)/2 is exact form. Let's check if this is the correct answer.

Alternatively, maybe we can verify algebraically:

Let me denote x=(√17 -1)/2. Let's check the original equation:

Left side: sqrt(4 - sqrt(4 - x)).

Compute 4 - x: 4 - (√17 -1)/2 = (8 - √17 +1)/2 = (9 - √17)/2.

Then sqrt(4 - x) = sqrt((9 - √17)/2).

Compute 4 - sqrt((9 - √17)/2):

Let me compute this step by step. Let’s denote A = sqrt((9 - √17)/2). Then 4 - A should equal x².

Wait, from the first equation when we squared both sides, we had 4 - sqrt(4 - x) = x². So:

x² = 4 - A, where A = sqrt(4 - x). So if x=(√17 -1)/2, let's compute x²:

x² = [(√17 -1)/2]^2 = (17 - 2√17 +1)/4 = (18 - 2√17)/4 = (9 - √17)/2.

So 4 - A = x² = (9 - √17)/2. Then A = 4 - (9 - √17)/2 = (8 -9 + √17)/2 = (-1 + √17)/2.

But A was sqrt((9 - √17)/2). Let's check if sqrt((9 - √17)/2) equals (-1 + √17)/2. Wait, sqrt of something is positive, and (-1 + √17)/2 is positive because √17≈4.123, so 4.123 -1≈3.123, divided by 2≈1.5615. So both sides are positive. Let's square (-1 + √17)/2:

[(-1 + √17)/2]^2 = (1 - 2√17 +17)/4 = (18 - 2√17)/4 = (9 - √17)/2.

Which is indeed equal to the expression under the square root in A. Therefore, the equality holds. Therefore, x=(√17 -1)/2 is indeed a solution.

Therefore, the solution is x=(√17 -1)/2.

**Final Answer**
The solution is \boxed{\dfrac{\sqrt{17} - 1}{2}}.
</think>To solve the equation \(\sqrt{4 - \sqrt{4 - x}} = x\) for \(x > 0\), we start by squaring both sides to eliminate the outer square root:

\[
(\sqrt{4 - \sqrt{4 - x}})^2 = x^2 \implies 4 - \sqrt{4 - x} = x^2
\]

Next, we isolate the remaining square root:

\[
4 - x^2 = \sqrt{4 - x}
\]

We square both sides again to remove the square root:

\[
(4 - x^2)^2 = (\sqrt{4 - x})^2 \implies (4 - x^2)^2 = 4 - x
\]

Expanding and simplifying the left side:

\[
16 - 8x^2 + x^4 = 4 - x
\]

Rearranging all terms to one side:

\[
x^4 - 8x^2 + x + 12 = 0
\]

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;

int main() {
    int n, k;
    cin >> n >> k;
    vector<int> w(n);
    int a = 0, b = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> w[i];
        if (w[i] == 50) a++;
        else b++;
    }

    for (int x : w) {
        if (x > k) {
            cout << "-1\n0\n";
            return 0;
        }
    }

    vector<vector<long long>> comb(51, vector<long long>(51, 0));
    comb[0][0] = 1;
    for (int i = 1; i <= 50; ++i) {
        comb[i][0] = 1;
        comb[i][i] = 1;
        for (int j = 1; j < i; ++j) {
            comb[i][j] = (comb[i-1][j-1] + comb[i-1][j]) % MOD;
        }
    }

    int initial_a = a, initial_b = b;
    int dist[51][51][2];
    long long cnt[51][51][2];
    memset(dist, -1, sizeof(dist));
    queue<tuple<int, int, int>> q;

    dist[a][b][0] = 0;
    cnt[a][b][0] = 1;
    q.emplace(a, b, 0);

    while (!q.empty()) {
        auto [curr_a, curr_b, pos] = q.front();
        q.pop();
        int curr_dist = dist[curr_a][curr_b][pos];
        long long curr_cnt = cnt[curr_a][curr_b][pos];

        if (pos == 0) {
            for (int x = 0; x <= curr_a; ++x) {
                for (int y = 0; y <= curr_b; ++y) {
                    if (x + y == 0 || 50*x + 100*y > k) continue;
                    int na = curr_a - x, nb = curr_b - y;
                    int np = 1;
                    long long ways = (comb[curr_a][x] * comb[curr_b][y]) % MOD;
                    if (dist[na][nb][np] == -1) {
                        dist[na][nb][np] = curr_dist + 1;
                        cnt[na][nb][np] = (curr_cnt * ways) % MOD;
                        q.emplace(na, nb, np);
                    } else if (dist[na][nb][np] == curr_dist + 1) {
                        cnt[na][nb][np] = (cnt[na][nb][np] + curr_cnt * ways) % MOD;
                    }
                }
            }
        } else {
            int other_a = initial_a - curr_a;
            int other_b = initial_b - curr_b;
            for (int x = 0; x <= other_a; ++x) {
                for (int y = 0; y <= other_b; ++y) {
                    if (x + y == 0 || 50*x + 100*y > k) continue;
                    int na = curr_a + x, nb = curr_b + y;
                    int np = 0;
                    long long ways = (comb[other_a][x] * comb[other_b][y]) % MOD;
                    if (dist[na][nb][np] == -1) {
                        dist[na][nb][np] = curr_dist + 1;
                        cnt[na][nb][np] = (curr_cnt * ways) % MOD;
                        q.emplace(na, nb, np);
                    } else if (dist[na][nb][np] == curr_dist + 1) {
                        cnt[na][nb][np] = (cnt[na][nb][np] + curr_cnt * ways) % MOD;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    if (dist[0][0][1] == -1) {
        cout << "-1\n0\n";
    } else {
        cout << dist[0][0][1] << "\n" << cnt[0][0][1] % MOD << "\n";
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>[
4 - x^2 = \sqrt{4 - x}
\]

We square both sides again to remove the square root:

\[
(4 - x^2)^2 = (\sqrt{4 - x})^2 \implies (4 - x^2)^2 = 4 - x
\]

Expanding and simplifying the left side:

\[
16 - 8x^2 + x^4 = 4 - x
\]

Rearranging all terms to one side:

\[
x^4 - 8x^2 + x + 12 = 0
\]

We factor the quartic equation into two quadratics. Let's denote the quartic as:

\[
x^4 - 8x^2 + x + 12 = (x^2 + ax + b)(x^2 + cx + d)
\]

Expanding the product:

\[
(x^2 + ax + b)(x^2 + cx + d) = x^4 + (a + c)x^3 + (ac + b + d)x^2 + (ad + bc)x + bd
\]

Matching coefficients with \(x^4 - 8x^2 + x + 12\):

1. Coefficient of \(x^3\): \(a + c = 0 \implies c = -a\)
2. Coefficient of \(x^2\): \(ac + b + d = -8 \implies -a^2 + b + d = -8\)
3. Coefficient of \(x\): \(ad + bc = 1 \implies a(d - b) = 1\)
4. Constant term: \(bd = 12\)

From \(a(d - b) = 1\) and \(bd = 12\), we test integer values for \(a\):

- If \(a = 1\), then \(d - b = 1\) and \(bd = 12\). Solving \(b(d - b) = 12\) with \(d = b + 1\):

\[
b(b + 1) = 12 \implies b^2 + b - 12 = 0 \implies b = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 48}}{2} = \frac{-1 \pm 7}{2}
\]

Thus, \(b = 3\) or \(b = -4\).

- If \(b = 3\), then \(d = 4\). Substituting into \(-a^2 + b + d = -8\):

\[
-1 + 3 + 4 = 6 \neq -8 \quad \text{(not valid)}
\]

- If \(b = -4\), then \(d = -3\). Substituting into \(-a^2 + b + d = -8\):

\[
-1 - 4 - 3 = -8 \quad \text{(valid)}
\]

Thus, the factorization is:

\[
(x^2 + x - 4)(x^2 - x - 3) = 0
\]

Solving each quadratic equation:

1. \(x^2 + x - 4 = 0\):

\[
x = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 16}}{2} = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{17}}{2}
\]

2. \(x^2 - x - 3 = 0\):

\[
x = \frac{1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 12}}{2} = \frac{1 \pm \sqrt{13}}{2}
\]

Considering \(x > 0\), we discard the negative solutions:

- \(x = \frac{-1 + \sqrt{17}}{2}\)
- \(x = \frac{1 + \sqrt{13}}{2}\)

We check these solutions in the original equation:

- For \(x = \frac{-1 + \sqrt{17}}{2}\):

\[
\sqrt{4 - \sqrt{4 - \left(\frac{-1 + \sqrt{17}}{2}\right)}} = \frac{-1 + \sqrt{17}}{2}
\]

This solution satisfies the equation.

- For \(x = \frac{1 + \sqrt{13}}{2}\):

\[
\sqrt{4 - \sqrt{4 - \left(\frac{1 + \sqrt{13}}{2}\right)}} \neq \frac{1 + \sqrt{13}}{2}
\]

This solution does not satisfy the equation.

Thus, the valid solution is:

\[
\boxed{\frac{\sqrt{17} - 1}{2}}
\]<#sep#> nodes to the first k).

## Summary of issue
When installing with pip, the data files used in examples are not present.

## Code for reproducing the issue
From a fresh install using python [`venv`](https://docs.python.org/3.5/library/venv.html):
```
$ python -m venv serpentTools
$ source serpentTools/bin/activate
(serpentTools) python -m pip install serpentTools
(serpentTools) python -c "import serpentTools; serpentTools.readDataFile('demo.coe')
```
## Actual outcome including console output and error traceback if applicable
```OSError: File matching demo.coe does not exist```
## Expected outcome
This doesn't fail
## Versions

* Version  from ``serpentTools.__version__`` ``0.6.1``

* Python version - ``python --version`` ``3.7.2``

• constant. The moment of inertia is I = bh 3/12, and the area is A = bh. Then I = h(bh2)/12 = cb (bh2)/12 = (c/12)(bh)2 = CA 2, where C = c/12 (a constant). Thus, Eq. (2-27) becomes 1/23 3 klA CE        and Eq. (2-29) becomes 1/2 5/2 1/23 km Al l C E               Thus, minimize  3 1/2f M E   , or maximize 1/2EM   . From Fig. (2-16) From the list of materials given, aluminum alloys are clearly the best followed by steels and tungsten carbide. Polycarbonate polymer is not a good choice compared to the other candidate materials. #include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

vector<ll> compute_prefix(const vector<int>& items) {
    vector<ll> prefix = {0};
    for (int x : items) {
        prefix.push_back(prefix.back() + x);
    }
    return prefix;
}

ll get_value(int R, int j, const vector<ll>& sum2, const vector<ll>& sum1, int cnt1) {
    if (j < 0 || j >= (int)sum2.size()) return 0;
    int remaining = R - 2 * j;
    if (remaining < 0) return 0;
    int take1 = min(remaining, cnt1);
    return sum2[j] + sum1[take1];
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    vector<int> w1, w2, w3;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int w, c;
        cin >> w >> c;
        if (w == 1) w1.push_back(c);
        else if (w == 2) w2.push_back(c);
        else w3.push_back(c);
    }

    sort(w1.rbegin(), w1.rend());
    sort(w2.rbegin(), w2.rend());
    sort(w3.rbegin(), w3.rend());

    vector<ll> sum1 = compute_prefix(w1);
    vector<ll> sum2 = compute_prefix(w2);
    vector<ll> sum3 = compute_prefix(w3);

    int cnt1 = w1.size();
    int cnt2 = w2.size();
    int cnt3 = w3.size();

    vector<ll> dp(m + 1, 0);

    for (int R = 0; R <= m; ++R) {
        ll case2 = 0;

        if (R >= cnt1) {
            int j_max_case2 = (R - cnt1) / 2;
            j_max_case2 = min(j_max_case2, cnt2);
            if (j_max_case2 >= 0) {
                ll s2 = sum2[j_max_case2];
                ll s1 = sum1[cnt1];
                case2 = s2 + s1;
            }
        }

        ll case1 = 0;

        int j_low = max(0, (R - cnt1 + 1) / 2);
        int j_high = min(R / 2, cnt2);

        if (j_low <= j_high) {
            int low = j_low;
            int high = j_high;
            int best_j = j_low;

            while (low <= high) {
                if (low == high) {
                    best_j = low;
                    break;
                }
                if (high - low == 1) {
                    ll val_low = get_value(R, low, sum2, sum1, cnt1);
                    ll val_high = get_value(R, high, sum2, sum1, cnt1);
                    if (val_high > val_low) {
                        best_j = high;
                    } else {
                        best_j = low;
                    }
                    break;
                }
                int mid = (low + high) / 2;
                ll val_mid = get_value(R, mid, sum2, sum1, cnt1);
                ll val_mid1 = get_value(R, mid + 1, sum2, sum1, cnt1);
                if (val_mid < val_mid1) {
                    low = mid + 1;
                } else {
                    high = mid;
                }
            }

            case1 = get_value(R, best_j, sum2, sum1, cnt1);
        }

        dp[R] = max(case1, case2);
    }

    ll max_total = 0;
    int max_c = min(cnt3, m / 3);
    for (int c = 0; c <= max_c; ++c) {
        int rem_weight = m - 3 * c;
        if (rem_weight < 0) break;
        ll current = sum3[c] + (rem_weight >= 0 ? dp[rem_weight] : 0);
        max_total = max(max_total, current);
    }

    cout << max_total << endl;

    return 0;

+#!/bin/bash
+# Install script that depends on $INSTALL variable
+# Options:
+# setup: install with setup.py
+# sdist: create a source distribution and install from that
+# bdist_wheel: create a wheel and install from that
+
+if [[ -z $ST_INSTALL ]]; then exit; fi
+
+set -ev
+set -o pipefail
+
+ST_VERSION=$(python setup.py version | grep Version | cut -d" " -f 2)
+echo $ST_VERSION
+
+case $ST_INSTALL in
+    'setup')
+        python setup.py install
+        ;;
+    'sdist')
+        python setup.py sdist
+        pip install dist/serpentTools-${ST_VERSION}.tar.gz
+        ;;
+    'bdist_wheel')
+        python setup.py bdist_wheel
+        easy_install dist/serpentTools-${ST_VERSION}*whl
+        ;;
+    'bdist_egg')
+        python setup.py bdist_egg
+        easy_install dist/serpentTools-${ST_VERSION}*egg
+        ;;
+    *)
+        echo No install option
+        exit 1
+esac

However, for any cross section shape it can be shown that I = CA 2, where C is a constant. For example, consider a rectangular section of height h and width b, where for a given scaled shape, h = cb, where c is a constant. The moment of inertia is I = bh 3/12, and the area is A = bh. Then I = h(bh2)/12 = cb (bh2)/12 = (c/12)(bh)2 = CA 2, where C = c/12. Thus, Eq. (2-27) becomes 1/23 3 klA CE        and Eq. #include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 998244353;

struct FenwickTree {
    vector<int> tree;
    int n;

    FenwickTree(int size) : n(size), tree(size + 2, 0) {}

    void update(int idx, int delta) {
        for (; idx <= n; idx += idx & -idx)
            tree[idx] += delta;
    }

    int query(int idx) {
        int res = 0;
        for (; idx > 0; idx -= idx & -idx)
            res += tree[idx];
        return res;
    }
};

long long mod_pow(long long a, long long b) {
    long long res = 1;
    while (b > 0) {
        if (b % 2 == 1)
            res = res * a % MOD;
        a = a * a % MOD;
        b /= 2;
    }
    return res;
}

int main() {
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<int> p(n);
    vector<int> known_values;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        cin >> p[i];
        if (p[i] != -1) {
            known_values.push_back(p[i]);
        }
    }

    set<int> known_set(known_values.begin(), known_values.end());
    vector<int> S;
    for (int x = 1; x <= n; x++) {
        if (known_set.find(x) == known_set.end()) {
            S.push_back(x);
        }
    }
    sort(S.begin(), S.end());
    int m = S.size();

    int sum_known = 0;
    int k = known_values.size();
    if (k > 0) {
        vector<int> sorted = known_values;
        sort(sorted.begin(), sorted.end());
        map<int, int> rank;
        for (int i = 0; i < sorted.size(); i++) {
            rank[sorted[i]] = i + 1;
        }
        FenwickTree ft(sorted.size());
        for (int x : known_values) {
            int r = rank[x];
            int current = ft.query(ft.n);
            sum_known = (sum_known + (current - ft.query(r))) % MOD;
            ft.update(r, 1);
        }
    }

    vector<int> prefix(n + 1, 0);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        prefix[i+1] = prefix[i] + (p[i] == -1 ? 1 : 0);
    }

    long long sum_cross = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (p[i] == -1) continue;
        int x = p[i];
        int left = prefix[i];
        int right = prefix[n] - prefix[i+1];

        auto it_less = lower_bound(S.begin(), S.end(), x);
        int c_less = it_less - S.begin();

        auto it_greater = upper_bound(S.begin(), S.end(), x);
        int c_greater = S.end() - it_greater;

        sum_cross = (sum_cross + 1LL * left * c_greater) % MOD;
        sum_cross = (sum_cross + 1LL * right * c_less) % MOD;
    }
    if (m != 0) {
        long long inv_m = mod_pow(m, MOD - 2);
        sum_cross = sum_cross * inv_m % MOD;
    }

    long long sum_unknown = 0;
    if (m >= 2) {
        long long inv_4 = mod_pow(4, MOD - 2);
        sum_unknown = (1LL * m * (m - 1)) % MOD;
        sum_unknown = sum_unknown * inv_4 % MOD;
    }

    long long total = (sum_known + sum_cross + sum_unknown) % MOD;
    cout << total << endl;

    return 0;

But doing this naively may be O(n) per check.

So let me think about the steps:

Sorting the nodes: once in the binary search, per check, we have to mark which nodes are included in S (those with a_i >=x).

But even for each check, when x is given, you can:

Initialize an array or boolean array of which nodes are in S.

Then, perform a DFS or BFS to calculate the maximum connected component.

Yes.

But since each time the tree is the same, but which nodes are active (in the induced subgraph)

This sounds manageable.

So let me structure this.

The references:

- Each edge is between two nodes, in the tree.

- Any path in the induced subgraph S can only go through nodes in S and edges connecting them.

Now, in a tree, any induced subgraph of connected components are subtrees. For example, a connected component is a tree's subtree, connected via nodes in S.

To find connected components in the induced subgraph:

Perhaps, we can consider the tree structure and represent nodes in S and find connected components.

An efficient way would be:

- Do a BFS/DFS, and whenever we encounter a node in S that hasn't been visited, traverse all its adjacent nodes which are in S, counting the size of the component.

We can do this as follows:

Initialize visited array as false for all nodes.

Initialize max_size to 0.

for each node in 1..n:

    if not visited and a[i] >= x:

        perform BFS/DFS from this node, only visiting nodes in S,

        the size of this component is the current count,

        if this is larger than current max_size, update max_size.

Thus:

Each check(x) takes O(n) time.

The total time of the algorithm is O(n log(max a)), which should be O(n log(max Ai))= n*20.

Given n is 2e5, that's about 4e6 steps, which is acceptable.

Now, what about variables? The initial edges... need to represent the tree structure as adjacency lists.

So the plan:

Read all the data.

Sort the a array to facilitate binary search? Not exactly, perhaps store the a array as per the nodes.

Implement the algorithm steps.

Edge cases:

In the first example:

Input nodes are:

5 nodes, a's are [3,6,1,4,2]

Wait the nodes are 1-5. The a array is given as 3 6 1 4 2.

Wait:

node2: 6,

node3:1,

node4:4,

node5:2.

Checking x=3. The nodes >=3 are nodes 1 (3), 2(6),4(4). 

These nodes form a subgraph. 

Original tree edges are:

1 connected to 2, 3.

2 connected to 4,5. (original edges are: first edge 1-2, then 2-4, 2-5, etc.)

The tree is given by:

So nodes 2 (a=6), node 1(a3), node4(a4). The induced subgraph of x=3 would have nodes 1,2,4.

Looking at edges between them:

1 and 2 are connected, so node2 is connected to node1 and node4 (since those a_i >=3? 2's a_i is6 >=3, yes. node4 is a4 >=3 yes. node1 is 3.

Thus in the induced graph:

edges in the active nodes form:

1-2, 2-4. Thus the connected component of node1-2-4 is size 3. 

The component size is 3 which equals k=3. So the check passes, so x=3 is acceptable. 

So in this case, the maximum is thus 3.

Which matches the first example.

So the approach works.

Another test case:

Second example:

k=2.

So, when x=5. Let's see: the nodes with a>=5 are nodes 2,3,4. So the count is 3 nodes. 

Now, the original edges:

Edges are 1-2, 1-3, 1-4.

The subgraph with these nodes (2,3,4) are all connected via node1, but node1 a is1.

But the nodes in S are only nodes2,3,4, which are each connected only through node1, which is not part of S.

Thus the induced subgraph between nodes 2,3,4 are not connected (as their common neighbor 1 is not part of S's nodes (since their a is 5, but node1 has a1).

So the edges between those nodes must exist in the original tree edges between S's nodes. 

edge between nodes 2 and 3? no. Node2 is connected to 1, and node3 is connected to 1. So no edge between any of 2,3,4 except through node1.

Therefore, the induced subgraph between nodes 2-3-4 is disconnected, split into isolated nodes. So each is a component of size 1. 

 Thus when x=5, the maximum component is size 1 < k=2, so x=5 is not acceptable. So the check returns false. So binary search can go down.

Next, when x=1, condition is:

The nodes in S are all nodes (1's a is1 >=1, and others are 5 >=1).

The induced subgraph is the entire tree, which is connected. The component size is4 >=2. So yes. So the possible x can be up to higher.

The maximum possible is when x is 1.

Wait but the required minimal a is 1 (node1 is in first two nodes).

Wait but in the example, the correct answer is 1. The binary search would find that x=1 passes, but higher x like 5 is not possible (as when x=5, max component was 1 < 2).

The answer would then be 1, which is correct.

Hence the algorithm holds.

Thus the outline for the code is:

- Read the tree, build the adjacency list.

- Read the a array.

- Binary search on possible x.

- For each x:

   - Collect all nodes with a_i >=x. 

   - Find the maximum size of a connected component in the induced subgraph (using BFS or DFS)

   - check if that maximum size >=k.

- Keep track of the best x.

Now let's think about implementing this in code.

First, to handle the edges: since the tree is undirected, we can build an adjacency list.

Now, for each x in binary search, the code needs to:

- Precompute which nodes are "active" (a_i >=x). 

But to do this efficiently, for each node, we can store the a[i], then for a given x, iterate all nodes once and note whether they are active.

Then perform BFS or DFS over these active nodes.

Now, since the tree is connected, but the induced subgraph may be disconnected.

Implementing the BFS:

Initialize a visited array of size n.

Then, for each node from 1 to n:

if not visited and a[i] >=x:

   then start a BFS here.

   all the nodes reachable from this node via edges where both ends are in S (must traverse through nodes that are active).

Wait but in the induced subgraph, the edges between two nodes in S are present, so when moving between nodes:

The adjacency list contains all edges. For the BFS, whenever moving from a node u in S, you can go to any of its neighbors if they are also in S and not visited yet.

Thus, the BFS must only move through adjacent nodes that are part of the active set.

Thus, code steps (simplified):

def check(x, edges, a, n, k):

    active = [False]*(n+1) # 1-based.

    for i in 1..n:

        active[i] = (a[i] >=x)

    visited = [False]*(n+1)

    max_size = 0

    for each node in 1..n:

        if active[i] and not visited[i]:

            q = queue()

            q.add(i)

            visited[i]=True

            current_size =0

            while q not empty:

                u = q.pop()

                current_size +=1

                for v in adj[u]:

                    if active[v] and not visited[v]:

                        visited[v] =True

                        q.add(v)

            if current_size > max_size:

                max_size = current_size

            if max_size >=k:

                return True

    return max_size >=k

This is O(n) for each check<#sep#>Universes in results reader are not zero-indexed
## Summary of issue
The first index for universes is 1, likely taken directly from the result file burnup index. This misconstrues some of the style and pythonicness of the layout, as python arrays start at 0.

## Code for reproducing the issue
Read a result file with burnup and view the `universes` attribute

## Expected outcome
Universe burnup indexes start at 0

## Versions

* Version  from ``serpentTools.__version__`` 0.6.1

* Python version - ``python --version`` N/A

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;
const int inv2 = 500000004; // Modular inverse of 2 under MOD

void add_to_basis(vector<long long>& basis, long long x) {
    for (int i = 59; i >= 0; --i) {
        if ((x >> i) & 1) {
            if (basis[i] == 0) {
                basis[i] = x;
                break;
            } else {
                x ^= basis[i];
            }
        }
    }
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    vector<vector<pair<int, long long>>> adj(n + 1);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        long long t;
        cin >> u >> v >> t;
        adj[u].emplace_back(v, t);
        adj[v].emplace_back(u, t);
    }

    vector<bool> visited(n + 1, false);
    vector<long long> xor_val(n + 1, 0);
    long long total = 0;

    for (int u = 1; u <= n; ++u) {
        if (!visited[u]) {
            queue<int> q;
            q.push(u);
            visited[u] = true;
            xor_val[u] = 0;
            vector<long long> basis(60, 0);
            vector<long long> xors;
            xors.push_back(xor_val[u]);

            while (!q.empty()) {
                int curr = q.front();
                q.pop();
                for (auto [v, t] : adj[curr]) {
                    if (!visited[v]) {
                        visited[v] = true;
                        xor_val[v] = xor_val[curr] ^ t;
                        xors.push_back(xor_val[v]);
                        q.push(v);
                    } else {
                        long long cycle_xor = xor_val[curr] ^ t ^ xor_val[v];
                        add_to_basis(basis, cycle_xor);
                    }
                }
            }

            long long mask_basis = 0;
            int k = 0;
            for (long long vec : basis) {
                mask_basis |= vec;
                if (vec != 0) k++;
            }

            long long term1_sum = 0;
            int c = xors.size();
            for (int j = 0; j < 60; ++j) {
                if (!(mask_basis & (1LL << j))) {
                    int cnt = 0;
                    for (long long x : xors) {
                        if ((x >> j) & 1) cnt++;
                    }
                    long long a = cnt;
                    long long contrib = (a * (c - a)) % MOD;
                    contrib = contrib * ((1LL << j) % MOD) % MOD;
                    term1_sum = (term1_sum + contrib) % MOD;
                }
            }

            long long pow2k = 1;
            for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
                pow2k = (pow2k * 2) % MOD;
            }
            long long term1 = term1_sum * pow2k % MOD;

            long long mask_mod = mask_basis % MOD;
            long long pow2k_1 = (k == 0) ? 0 : (pow2k * inv2) % MOD;
            long long pairs = (1LL * c * (c - 1) / 2) % MOD;
            long long term2 = mask_mod * pow2k_1 % MOD;
            term2 = term2 * pairs % MOD;

            total = (total + term1 + term2) % MOD;
        }
    }

    cout << total << endl;

    return 0;

Wait, maybe recursively applying this identity. For example, let's write cosx = sin2x/(2 sinx), and cos2x = sin4x/(2 sin2x), etc. Then the product up to n would be [sin2x/(2 sinx)] * [sin4x/(2 sin2x)] * [sin8x/(2 sin4x)] * ... * [sin(2^n x)/(2 sin2^{n-1}x))]. This telescopes! Let's check:

Each term in the numerator is sin2x, sin4x, sin8x, ..., sin(2^n x). The denominator terms are 2 sinx, 2 sin2x, 2 sin4x, ..., 2 sin2^{n-1}x. Therefore, all the intermediate terms cancel out. So the product is [sin(2^n x)] / [2^n sinx]. Wait, this is the same as before, but this only works if the arguments are doubling each time. However, in our original problem, the product is of cos(kx) for k=1 to n, which is cosx * cos2x * cos3x * ... * cosnx. The formula I just derived applies to the product of cos(2^{k}x), not cos(kx). So this approach doesn't help for the given problem. That was my mistake earlier.

Therefore, that telescoping product works when each cosine has an angle that's double the previous, but not for linearly increasing angles. So this identity doesn't apply here. Hence, the product of cos(kx) from k=1 to n doesn't telescope like that. 

So, perhaps there's no general closed-form expression for the product of cos(kx) from 1 to n, which means that 1 - product_{k=1}^n cos(kx) can't be simplified further. Therefore, the answer might just be 1 minus the product of the cosines, as written. But the problem seems to suggest that there is a specific value, so maybe I'm missing something.

Wait, let me check the original problem again. It says: "What is the value of the product 1 - cos x cos 2x cos 3x cos 4x ... cos nx?" Wait, the word "product" here might be ambiguous. Is the entire expression 1 - [cosx cos2x ... cosnx] considered as a product, or is the problem asking for the value of that expression? If it's the former, maybe there's a misinterpretation. But 1 - product_{k=1}^n cos(kx) is indeed an expression, not a product. So perhaps the problem is just asking for the value of 1 minus that product of cosines. But how to express that in a simplified form?

Alternatively, perhaps the problem is a trick question. For example, when x=0, the expression is 0. When x=π, the product of cos(kπ) alternates between 1 and -1, depending on k. But unless there's a specific x given, I don't see how to find a numerical value. But the problem doesn't specify x, so the answer must be expressed in terms of x and n.

Wait, but the problem says "What is the value of the product...". If it's a product, maybe the user made a mistake and meant "expression" instead of "product". Assuming that, then the expression 1 - product_{k=1}^n cos(kx) can't be simplified into a more elementary form unless there's a specific identity I'm missing. Therefore, the answer would just be 1 - cosx cos2x ... cosnx. But that seems too trivial. Maybe the problem is expecting a different approach.

Wait, wait! I recall that in some cases, products of cosines can be represented using Chebyshev polynomials. Chebyshev polynomials of the first kind are defined as T_n(cosθ) = cos(nθ). But I don't see how this relates directly to a product of multiple cosines.

Alternatively, consider the Fourier transform approach. The product of cosines can be represented as a sum of cosines with different frequencies. But again, this might not help in simplifying the expression 1 minus that product.

Alternatively, think of small n cases:

n=1: 1 - cosx. Which is 2 sin²(x/2).

n=2: 1 - cosx cos2x. Maybe express this as 1 - cosx(2cos²x -1). Wait, cos2x = 2cos²x -1. So substituting, we get 1 - cosx(2cos²x -1) = 1 - 2cos³x + cosx. Then group terms: (1 + cosx) - 2cos³x. Hmm, but 1 + cosx = 2cos²(x/2). And 2cos³x = 2cosx * cos²x. Not sure if this helps. Alternatively, factor:

1 - cosx cos2x = 1 - cosx(2cos²x -1) = 1 - 2cos³x + cosx = (1 + cosx) - 2cos³x. Perhaps factor further: (1 + cosx) - 2cos³x = (1 + cosx) - 2cosx(cos²x). But cos²x = (1 + cos2x)/2. So substitution gives (1 + cosx) - 2cosx*(1 + cos2x)/2 = (1 + cosx) - cosx(1 + cos2x) = 1 + cosx - cosx - cosx cos2x = 1 - cosx cos2x. Which brings us back to the original expression. So this approach doesn't help.

Wait, maybe integrating or differentiating? If I consider the derivative of the product, but I'm not sure.

Alternatively, think of the product of cos(kx) as the real part of the product of exponentials. So, product_{k=1}^n cos(kx) = Re[ product_{k=1}^n e^{ikx} ] = Re[ e^{i x (1 + 2 + ... +n)} ] = Re[ e^{i x n(n+1)/2} ] = cos( x n(n+1)/2 ). But wait, this is only if all the terms are added, but in reality, the product of exponentials would be e^{i x(1 + 2 + ... +n)} only if all the exponents are added. But the product of e^{ikx} is e^{i x (1 + 2 + ... +n)}. However, when expanding the product of (e^{ikx} + e^{-ikx})/2, we get more terms. Hence, this approach only gives the real part of the sum of exponents. But the original product of cosines is more complicated. So the real part is only part of the story, but the product actually includes all combinations of + and - signs in the exponents. Therefore, this approach is not helpful.

Alternatively, if I multiply all the cosines, the product is the real part of the product of exponentials, but with all possible combinations. So the product is 2^{-n} times the sum over all subsets of {1, 2, ..., n} of exp(i x sum_{k in subset} k - i x sum_{k not in subset} k). Which simplifies to 2^{-n} sum_{S} exp(i x [sum_{k in S} k - sum_{k not in S} k]). Let me define T = sum_{k in S} k - sum_{k not in S} k. Then T = 2 sum_{k in S}k - sum_{k=1}^n k. Let the total sum be S_total = n(n+1)/2. So T = 2 sum_{S} k - S_total. Therefore, the exponent becomes i x (2 sum_S k - S_total). Hence, the product is 2^{-n} e^{-i x S_total} sum_{S} e^{i x 2 sum_S k}. But this is still a complicated expression. There's no obvious simplification here for general n.

Given all these attempts and not finding a standard identity or simplification, I might conclude that there's no general closed-form expression for 1 - product_{k=1}^n cos(kx). Therefore, the answer is simply 1 - cosx cos2x ... cosnx, and it can't be simplified further without additional constraints on x or n.

So, the check function can be written as follows:

def check(x):

    active = [False] * (n+1)

    for i in range(1, n+1):

        if array_a[i] >= x:

            active[i] = True

    visited = [False]*(n+1)

    for i in range(1, n+1):

        if active[i] and not visited[i]:

            q = deque()

            q.append(i)

            visited[i] = True

            size =0

            while q:

                u = q.popleft()

                size +=1

                for v in adj[u]:

                    if active[v] and not visited[v]:

                        visited[v] = True

                        q.append(v)

            if size >=k:

                return True

    return False

Wait, wait in code above, when after doing a BFS of component starting at i, it computes the size, and if that size is >=k, return True.

Because we can just find the first component with size >=k and return immediately. 

This optimizes the time, because once a component is found with such size, we don't need to process other components. 

Yes, this is better.

Thus, this function returns true as soon as any component has size >=k, or false only if all components are below k.

Thus, the code runs faster.

This is better.

#include <vector>
#include <iostream>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

int main() {
    int n;
    cin >> n;

    vector<bool> used(n + 1, false);
    vector<int> primes;

    if (n >= 2) {
        primes.push_back(2);
        vector<bool> is_prime(n + 1, true);
        is_prime[0] = is_prime[1] = false;
        for (int i = 3; i <= n; i += 2) {
            if (is_prime[i]) {
                primes.push_back(i);
                for (int j = i * i; j <= n; j += 2 * i) {
                    is_prime[j] = false;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    vector<int> evens;
    for (int i = 2; i <= n; i += 2) {
        evens.push_back(i);
    }
    int m_even = evens.size();
    vector<pair<int, int>> pairs;

    if (m_even >= 1) {
        if (m_even % 2 == 0) {
            for (int i = 0; i < m_even; i += 2) {
                pairs.emplace_back(evens[i], evens[i + 1]);
            }
            for (int x : evens) {
                used[x] = true;
            }
        } else {
            for (int i = 0; i < m_even - 1; i += 2) {
                pairs.emplace_back(evens[i], evens[i + 1]);
            }
            for (int i = 0; i < m_even - 1; ++i) {
                used[evens[i]] = true;
            }
        }
    }

    int max_even = (m_even % 2 == 1) ? evens.back() : -1;

    for (size_t i = 1; i < primes.size(); ++i) {
        int p = primes[i];
        vector<int> multiples;
        for (int x = p; x <= n; x += p) {
            if (!used[x]) {
                multiples.push_back(x);
            }
        }

        if (multiples.empty()) continue;

        if (multiples.size() % 2 == 0) {
            for (size_t j = 0; j < multiples.size(); j += 2) {
                pairs.emplace_back(multiples[j], multiples[j + 1]);
                used[multiples[j]] = true;
                used[multiples[j + 1]] = true;
            }
        } else {
            bool has_max_even = (max_even != -1) && (max_even % p == 0) && !used[max_even];
            if (has_max_even) {
                multiples.push_back(max_even);
                used[max_even] = true;
                max_even = -1;
                for (size_t j = 0; j < multiples.size(); j += 2) {
                    pairs.emplace_back(multiples[j], multiples[j + 1]);
                    used[multiples[j]] = true;
                    used[multiples[j + 1]] = true;
                }
            } else {
                for (size_t j = 0; j < multiples.size() - 1; j += 2) {
                    pairs.emplace_back(multiples[j], multiples[j + 1]);
                    used[multiples[j]] = true;
                    used[multiples[j + 1]] = true;
                }
            }
        }
    }

    cout << pairs.size() << endl;
    for (auto& p : pairs) {
        cout << p.first << " " << p.second << endl;
    }

    return 0;

```python
import sys
from sys import stdin
from collections import deque

def main():
    sys.setrecursionlimit(1 << 25)
    n, k = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
    a = list(map(int, stdin.readline().split()))
    max_a = max(a)
    array_a = [0] * (n + 1)  # 1-based indexing
    for i in range(n):
        array_a[i+1] = a[i]
    
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
    for _ in range(n-1):
        u, v = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    
    def check(x):
        active = [False] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n+1):
            if array_a[i] >= x:
                active[i] = True
        visited = [False] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(1, n+1):
            if active[i] and not visited[i]:
                q = deque()
                q.append(i)
                visited[i] = True
                size = 0
                while q:
                    u = q.popleft()
                    size += 1
                    for v in adj[u]:
                        if active[v] and not visited[v]:
                            visited[v] = True
                            q.append(v)
                if size >= k:
                    return True
        return False
    
    low = 1
    high = max_a
    ans = 0
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        if check(mid):
            ans = mid
            low = mid + 1
        else:
            high = mid - 1
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
